# Progress Tracker — Form-Builder SaaS

**Single source of truth for "where are we right now."** Keep the top three sections
(Current Status · Next Session · the G table) current. Full per-session narrative history
lives in [PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md](PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md) and in git — do **not** re-read it to resume.

## Standing Rules

1. **Keep this tracker current** — update "Current Status" + "Next Session" as work completes. Never leave the repo in a state the next session has to reverse-engineer.
2. **One shared design system, no exceptions** — every page (builder, dashboard, inbox, settings, public runtime) uses the same app shell + component library + tokens (plan Main Feature #6 / DSR doc #19). Any exception needs a documented rationale.
3. **Fully styled by default** (confirmed 2026-07-05) — every page ships fully styled from shared components; only pure backend/API increments have no styling work. Don't re-ask "styled vs unstyled?" per increment.
4. **End every session** (confirmed 2026-07-10) with (a) a save to the MD files changed this session — this tracker's top sections + one dated line in `PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md`, plus any `docs/**`/ADR/plan actually touched — and (b) a copy-paste "what to prompt next" line. Keep it lean: update the top of this file + append one archive line; don't rewrite the whole history.

5. **Build everything buildable — the user's testing NEVER gates development** (confirmed 2026-08-06, and it SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-05 "leave every PR open until the app is testable" instruction). Their words: *"let's focus on all the tasks that can be made by you. I will test the app when it is completely built so it will not delay the development. I don't want any pending tasks to be missed."* So: never defer, park, or leave a PR unmerged because the user cannot verify it yet. Merge into the integration branch on green and keep going. `main` still receives nothing until the ONE final integration PR — that part of the original intent stands. **The only surviving blocker is an input the user alone possesses** (OCR scans; third-party OAuth credentials; a Stripe account; hardware), and even then, build every part that does not need it. A design decision the user has not made is NOT automatically a blocker: propose one, recommend, and proceed unless it is genuinely theirs (pricing, legal, an authorization widening).

- **I9a (as-built). `SubmissionStatus::ScreenedOut` + the `max_responses` capacity bug — the first of I9's THREE PRs** (split with the user 2026-08-08 on the I7a/I7b and I8a/b/c precedent: I9a ScreenedOut → I9b encode save-as-draft → I9c post-submission editing; the I-map itself said "likely I9a/I9b"). **It discharges `docs/workflow-branching-design.md:140`, a consequence that doc recorded and deliberately did not solve.**

  **⚠️ THE TRACKER'S OWN I9 SCOPE LINE WAS WRONG, AND ONLY READING THE SCHEMA FOUND IT — the I8 lesson, third occurrence.** The next-prompt block below said ScreenedOut needed an "enum + **CHECK migration**". There is no CHECK constraint on `submissions.status`: `2026_07_09_000301_create_submissions_table.php:35` is a plain `string('status', 20)->default('draft')`. **I9a ships NO migration at all.** The two partial indexes that DO hard-code `WHERE status <> 'draft'` were examined and deliberately left alone — `submissions_form_finalized_idx` serves the capacity COUNT *and* H24a's per-form series, so narrowing its predicate would have made it unusable for the series: a silent Risk-R9 regression no correctness test can see. The new capacity predicate is a strict superset, so the index is still usable and the extra tuples are rechecked on the scan.

  **⚠️ `screened_out` MEANS "WAS SHOWN NO QUESTIONS", NOT "WAS DISQUALIFIED", AND THE NAME PROMISES MORE THAN THE PREDICATE DELIVERS.** It is derived server-side by the new pure static `App\Support\Submissions\FinalizedStatus`, from `StepProjection::isEmpty()` over the answers being finalized — byte-for-byte the question the guest runtime already asks to decide it is rendering H21b's terminal panel, so no client flag and no new wire contract. Consequence, pinned by a test that asserts `submitted`: **an ordinary screener (the disqualifying question in a visible step) still burns a slot**, because the respondent saw it. Only the genuinely empty projection screens out — the H7 router arrived at bare, a section that hides itself, a form of nothing but hidden/calculated fields. **The narrower "the answers CAUSED the emptiness" reading was considered and REJECTED**: an H7 router form is empty-at-open by construction (which is why `StepGraphInspector::emptyAtOpen()` suppresses its publish notice for `PrefillSource::Url`), so that reading would classify the exact respondent doc #27 §4.1 describes as `submitted` and re-open the bug.

  **THE CAPACITY PREDICATE BECAME A NAMED SIBLING, AND ALL SIX SITES MOVED TOGETHER OR THE BANNER LIES.** New `Submission::scopeConsumesCapacity()` (not `draft`, not `screened_out`), adopted by `FormAcceptanceGuard::assertCapacity()` and by its two display twins — now `capacityCount()` in both presenters — plus the hand-spelled raw SQL in `AnalyticsMetricsService::acceptingFormsCount()` and the renamed `$consumedCount` through `FormSchedule`/`FormScheduleView`. Fixing the guard alone would have left the public runtime telling a respondent "capacity reached" on a form still accepting. **`scopeCountable()` is deliberately UNCHANGED**: a screened-out response is a real row the tenant received and the inbox lists it by default, and it is the predicate `submissions_analytics_series_idx` is partial on. ⚠️ **Two `!=` conjuncts, never `whereNotIn`** — `NOT IN` compiles to `<> ALL(...)`, from which Postgres is not guaranteed to prove `submissions_form_finalized_idx`'s `WHERE status <> 'draft'`, so the hot finalize COUNT under `lockForUpdate` could silently become a scan with every test green. A `toSql()` shape pin guards it.

  **⚠️ THE SEVENTH COPY OF THE PREDICATE WAS THE CAPACITY TEST'S OWN ORACLE**, and `AnalyticsMetricsServiceTest`'s parity case was VACUOUS for this change — its oracle was `countable()` and it seeded no screened-out row, so the two implementations could have diverged on exactly the new status while staying green. Both repointed; the parity case now seeds the row that makes it bite. **All four mutation checks were run and all four redden**: revert the guard predicate (3 failures), revert the analytics subquery (the parity case), delete `promote()`'s `$sections = $version->sections()->get();` (the submit/promote-agreement case *only* — the screen-out half still passes, which is why the agreement is asserted in BOTH directions), flip `FinalizedStatus`'s polarity (all 7 unit cases).

  **`Show.vue`'s archive gate became a POSITIVE list, and that is the durable half of the fix.** It read `s !== 'archived' && s !== 'draft'` while the server uses a positive `$from`, so the two agreed only by accident and every new status silently became archivable in the UI. Here that would have been worse than a dead button: **`archived` consumes a capacity slot and `screened_out` does not**, so the transition would have retroactively overfilled a paid cap. Paired PHP/Vitest sweeps now assert the offered set EQUALS the accepted set.

  **A 38-AGENT ADVERSARIAL REVIEW RAN BEFORE THE COMMIT, AND ITS YIELD WAS ALMOST ENTIRELY IN THE CLASS OF DEFECT A GREEN SUITE CANNOT SEE.** Five independent lenses over the working diff, every finding then handed to a verifier prompted to REFUTE it: 33 raised, 3 survived. **But the refuters were too aggressive, and taking their verdicts at face value would have shipped roughly a dozen real defects** — the failure mode was over-applying "that is a deliberate decision" and "a doc is not behaviour". Re-verifying by hand promoted these back: the published `docs/api-specification.md` and `docs/ux/design-system-reference.md` both still enumerated six statuses; `docs/TESTING-GUIDE.md`'s "known gaps" table still listed screened-out as unbuilt **and still listed I8's step-up/org-2FA as unbuilt, which I8 itself had left stale**; the capacity-index migration's docblock still quoted the guard's old query; ADR-0011 §D2 still declared "one countable-submission predicate" with no note that I9a had forked a second (amended, on the I7b/ADR-0002 §D3 precedent); and **three docblocks — one of them newly written by this increment — asserted "the live COUNT includes the row being finalized", which is now false precisely when the row is screened out.**

  **TWO REAL DEFECTS AND ONE VACUOUS TEST CAME OUT OF IT.** (1) `NotifyOnSubmissionCreated` was dispatching **`review_requested` for terminal screened-out rows** — sending a reviewer to a detail page where all four actions are refused, which is exactly how a bell teaches people to ignore it. `submission_received` still fires and the asymmetry is argued in the listener. (2) Both seeders wrote a **full answer document onto screened-out rows**, contradicting the state's own definition on the first page a tester would open to understand it. (3) **The "banner agrees with the guard" test was true by construction** — it called the pure `FormScheduleView::present()` and fed it a count it computed itself with the same scope, so it compared the scope to itself while its comment claimed it went "through the presenter's own wire block". It now goes through the real `PublicFormPresenter` and asserts the guard alongside. **The confirmed-and-uncontested finding is the one this project has now hit three times: a docblock cited `ConsumesCapacityScopeTest` as the guard against `whereNotIn`, and no such file exists** — the pin is real but lives in `ScreenedOutTest`, so a maintainer who checked would have found nothing and concluded the deterrent was never written. Same shape as I8's exceptions-log #6.

  **⚠️ SCRAMBLE PUBLISHES THE ENUM'S DOCBLOCK VERBATIM INTO `openapi.json`.** The first draft put the whole I9a argument in `/** */` and the regenerated spec grew a 2,000-character `description` full of `{@see}` tags and box-drawing rules — in a customer-facing contract under a byte-identity CI gate, where every future edit to internal prose would move the spec. The reasoning now lives in a plain `/* */` block comment (Scramble ignores those) and the docblock is the one paragraph an API reader needs. The committed diff is exactly two lines: the description sentence and the new enum value. **Case ORDER is a wire contract** — `ScreenedOut` sits beside `Submitted` because it is a finalize outcome, and moving it later is a spec diff.

  **⚠️ THE I-MAP'S DECLARED I8 SCOPE WAS WRONG IN THREE PLACES, AND ONLY READING THE CODE FOUND IT.** The plan listed "step-up on 4 route groups, org-2FA, **HIBP**, formula-injection prefix, auth pages into responsive-axe, **Blueprint dark bg-active fix**". Verified against the tree: **HIBP was already shipped** — `FortifyServiceProvider:42` has carried `Password::min(12)->uncompromised()` since Phase 0 B1 (`e4253fa`), inherited by registration, reset, profile-update and invitation-accept. **The Blueprint dark `bg-active` 2.52:1 failure was already fixed** in H21d1 (`774b9ae`) and guarded by `theme-overrides.test.ts` — `docs/ux/exceptions-log.md` #6 was simply never updated, and I8 planned a work item against stale prose. **And `password.confirm` did not guard "ZERO routes"**: `config/fortify.php`'s `confirmPassword` feature already applied it to seven Fortify 2FA endpoints; it guarded zero *application* routes, which is the real gap. **The generalisable lesson, now recorded in exceptions-log #6 itself: a "recorded so it is not rediscovered as new" note becomes an active liability the moment it outlives its subject — it is trusted precisely because it looks like diligence. Close the entry in the same commit that closes the thing.**

  **⚠️ I8a FOUND A LIVE DEFECT THAT WAS A LOCKOUT ON ONE PAGE, AND IT HAD NEVER SURFACED BECAUSE NOTHING TESTED IT.** `TwoFactorSetup.vue` read `res.json()` unconditionally on two JSON sidecars carrying `password.confirm`. **`RequirePassword` forks on `expectsJson()`**: a navigation is REDIRECTED, but a `fetch` with `Accept: application/json` gets a bare **423 with a JSON body**. So a lapsed confirmation window produced `qrSvg = undefined` and a non-array `recoveryCodes` — a blank QR under copy saying "scan this", no error in any log. The same component is the super-admin's *mandatory* enrolment page, where `superadmin.mfa` allows no other console route. There were **zero tests against any `/user/two-factor-*` endpoint**. Fixed with TWO guards on purpose — a server-computed `needs_password_confirmation` prop (so the fetch is never made) and a `res.ok` check (the window can lapse between render and click); deleting either leaves the other looking sufficient. **⚠️ NEVER MOUNT `step-up` ON A ROUTE A JSON SIDECAR CALLS** — that is this defect, one careless mount from returning; a manifest test states the negative half of the policy (`/notifications`, `members.index`, `admin.mfa.setup`) as explicitly as the positive half.

  **⚠️ 43 PRE-EXISTING CONSOLE TESTS BROKE, AND THAT IS THE COST OF THE GATE RATHER THAN A DEFECT IN IT.** PRD #14 names "the super-admin console" whole, and its READS are cross-tenant (every workspace's user list, every platform audit row), so `step-up` sits on the entire `superadmin.mfa` inner group rather than only its mutations. Six suites predate it. Paid once via `confirmPasswordNow()` in `tests/Pest.php`. **ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: `auth → superadmin → superadmin.mfa → step-up`, with step-up INSIDE the mfa group** — reversed, an un-enrolled operator confirms a password and is then bounced to enrolment having gained nothing, and a prompt that accomplishes nothing is how a control teaches people to click through it. `auth.step_up_timeout` is a SECOND config key (900s) rather than a change to `password_timeout` (10800s), which Fortify's own enrolment flow still needs.

  **`tenant.roles.assign` HAD BEEN SEEDED TO OWNER/ADMIN AND DOCUMENTED IN RBAC §5 SINCE PHASE 0 WITH ZERO CONSUMERS** — the same dormant-key situation I7a found on `feedback.view`. There was no role-change route at all, so PRD #14's "role changes" clause was vacuously satisfiable while `TenantMembershipService::joinOpenTenant()`'s docblock told invitees an Owner "can promote them on the Members page in two clicks". `PATCH /members/{user}/role` consumes the existing key rather than minting one; its four refusals (Owner's role immutable, `owner` unassignable, no self-re-grading, no-op refused) live in the SERVICE, because no FormRequest can know any of them.

  **⚠️ `SettingKey::SecurityRequireTwoFactor` DEFAULTS FALSE, WHICH IS NOT THE FAIL-CLOSED READING THE REST OF THAT ENUM REACHES FOR.** The table is sparse, so "absent" is every workspace that already exists: defaulting true would, on the deploy that adds the key, redirect every member of every workspace to an enrolment interstitial — including Owners, who would then have to enrol before reaching the switch to turn it off. **The interstitial is exempt STRUCTURALLY** (its own route group), not by a path allow-list inside the middleware — an allow-list would have to name `/settings` too, and every future `/settings/*` route would inherit an exemption nobody granted. An unenrolled Owner who switches it on **is gated out of `/settings` on the next request**: correct (a policy its author is exempt from is not a policy), pinned as a test, and warned about on the card.

  **RESPONDENT CLAUSE (deferred from I4, decided with the user 2026-08-07):** `SubmissionPolicy::view()` gains an `isRespondent()` arm — **`view()` ONLY**, never `review()` or `export()`; post-submission EDITING stays with I9. `NotificationPresenter` needed no change at all: it runs the real Gate, so its previously-dead links simply started resolving. **⚠️ A CLAIM I MADE AND MUTATION TESTING DISPROVED, kept because the comment would otherwise mislead:** the closure around `scopeVisibleTo()`'s `orWhere` does NOT protect the inbox's filters from OR-association — flattening it leaves the whole suite green, because `Builder::callScope()` already re-groups whatever a local scope adds. The closure stays (that protection belongs to the CALL MECHANISM and evaporates on a direct `scopeVisibleTo()` call), but the docblock now says the true thing rather than the plausible one.

  **I8b CLOSED PRD FEATURE #3'S LAST CRITERION WITH TWO MECHANISMS, NOT ONE.** Rate limiting bounds VELOCITY from one source and structurally cannot see low-and-slow distributed submission; a proof-of-work challenge costs the ATTACKER and bounds exactly that. **Both default off** — the threat model requires it, because M&E deployments run on trusted enumerator devices where a spam check is actively harmful UX. **NO hosted CAPTCHA**: each needs tenant-held credentials (which would have made this a *blocked* row), needs `script-src`+`frame-src` added to `PublicRuntimeSecurityHeaders` whose test asserts their ABSENCE as a tripwire, and cannot work offline-first. **A honeypot was rejected outright** — the guest ingest is a public documented JSON API whose body ships in `api-client.ts` and `openapi.json`, so a bot writes eight lines of curl and never parses a DOM.

  **⚠️ THE CHALLENGE IS SOLVED AT *SEND* TIME, NEVER AT FILL TIME, AND THAT IS WHAT KEEPS OFFLINE WORKING.** `replay.ts` already re-mints and re-resolves the schema immediately before every outbox POST, so a challenge minted 200 ms earlier cannot expire even for a row queued for days. Consequence: **`OutboxRow` gains no columns, `db.ts` needs no Dexie bump, and `outbox.ts`/`replay.ts`/`sw.ts`/`RuntimeSession.vue` are untouched.** Three more traps: **`crypto.subtle` is undefined in an insecure context in PRODUCTION** (secure-context spans the whole ancestor chain, and `frame-ancestors *` is deliberate per PRD:187), so `challenge.ts` carries a pure-JS SHA-256 pinned to NIST FIPS 180-4 vectors; the endpoint is **POST not GET**, because `sw.ts` NetworkFirst-caches GET on that prefix and a cached challenge is one the replay guard rejects; and it is **bound to the FORM, not the share token**, because a token binding would reject exactly the offline replays this product exists to support. The rate limit is **per-IP within one form, never form-wide** — a form-wide bucket is a SELF-DoS lever.

  **I8c CLOSED THE THREAT MODEL'S LAST `Open` ROW.** `App\Support\Export\SpreadsheetCell` prefixes a leading `=`/`+`/`-`/`@`/TAB/CR at all **seven** `Row::fromValues()` sites across **four** writers. **⚠️ The `is_numeric` guard is the half that would ship as a DATA-INTEGRITY bug** — `-5` and `+40` are ordinary answers, and prefixing them turns every negative figure in every export into literal text. **⚠️ NOT applied in `SubmissionRowProjector`**, which looks like the one seam covering everything: it is shared with `GoogleSheetsConnector`, already structurally safe via `valueInputOption=RAW`, so sanitising there would put a visible apostrophe in front of every value in a tenant's LIVE spreadsheet. **⚠️ I2's note pointed at the wrong column and the correction matters**: the audit export's `Changes` renders as `key: old → new`, so tenant data never starts that cell — the exposed column is **`Actor`**, a user's own display name.

  **DEFERRED, WITH THE REASONING RE-STATED RATHER THAN THE OLD ONE INHERITED:** the admin console still stays out of Playwright. The `otplib` blocker `docs/feature-backlog.md` §7 named has **dissolved** (TOTP is ~30 lines with node's `crypto`), but **I8a added a hop that did not exist when that row was filed** — every console page now carries `step-up`, so a console `globalSetup` must sequence login → TOTP → console route → confirm-password redirect → back. Four sequenced redirects in a setup file that cannot be exercised outside CI, and shipping unverified E2E infrastructure into a merge-blocking gate buys CI cycles rather than confidence. `auth-axe.spec.ts` DID land for the unauthenticated pages, using the proven per-file no-storageState idiom rather than the config restructure that row assumed; `/user/confirm-password` joined `responsive-axe` instead, since it sits behind `auth`.

- **I7b (as-built, MERGED into `phase1-completion` via PR #107 `c736220`, 6/6 CI GREEN on `ec8418b` — every job with real steps, 11–20 each, none `steps: []`; local Pest 2841 passed / 0 failed, Vitest 72 files / 1363 tests. The 2 Pest "warnings" are pre-existing and intentional — `StructuredRuleEvaluatorTest` feeds a malformed regex on purpose to prove `preg_match()` failure is treated as no-match).** **The console gained the depth its services already had, and closed the platform half of PRD Feature #12.** Three things that existed but could not be reached: `POST /admin/tenants/{tenant}/plan` + `SuperAdminService::assignPlan()` had shipped in **H5a with no UI at all**, reachable only by hand; there was no `/admin/tenants/{tenant}` page, so an operator could suspend a workspace but could not see its plan, usage or domains; and I5's platform-settings writes had been depositing `audits` rows with `tenant_id = NULL` since 2026-08-06 that **no surface in the product could read**. Now: a workspace detail page (identity + owner + plan-assign + usage + capabilities + read-only domains), the tenant list's rows finally link somewhere, and `GET /admin/audit-log` reads the platform ledger. **The RLS decision is the increment's centre of gravity** — the obvious implementation, `applySuperAdminBypass('audits', ['SELECT'])`, is one line and would have handed the operator **every tenant's complete history** to display a handful of settings rows; what shipped is a new NAMED generator `TenantIsolation::platformRowsBypass()` whose gate carries `AND tenant_id IS NULL`, so a tenant cannot read platform rows **and** the operator cannot read tenant rows. The plan said hand-write that policy; ADR-0002 §D2 says policies are generated, not hand-written, so it became a generator instead (user-approved fork) and ADR-0002 §D3 carries the amendment.

- **I7a (as-built, MERGED into `phase1-completion` via PR #106 `5b7bc7f`, 6/6 CI GREEN on `e840002` — every job with real steps, none `steps: []`; the clean local suite was 2780 passed / 0 failed).** **PRD Feature #11 is closed end-to-end.** It was **write-only**: two writers (a bare `FeedbackReport::create()` in a controller, and `DemoSeeder`) and **zero readers anywhere in the tree** — feedback a tester filed went into a table nobody could open. Now: native `getDisplayMedia` capture → the shared `attachments` table (`AttachmentKind::FeedbackScreenshot`, the 9th kind) → the workspace's own read-only `/feedback` list, which finally consumes the `feedback.view` permission seeded since Phase 0 with no code behind it → the platform support console at `/admin/feedback` with the New → Reviewed → Resolved lifecycle. **ADR-0015 closes the PRD's own explicit open decision** on capture technique (html2canvas renders the Leaflet picker and the signature canvas blank — the two surfaces a bug report is most likely to be about — and would add a ~1 MB prod dependency to what `npm audit --omit=dev` blocks on); the file input is rendered **always**, not as a failure fallback, so a decline costs the reporter nothing.

  **⚠️ THE DEFECT THE TESTS CAUGHT, AND IT WOULD NEVER HAVE ANNOUNCED ITSELF.** The super-admin RLS carve-out is only the DATABASE half. `BelongsToTenant` independently injects `where tenant_id = <context ?? SENTINEL>`, and the console runs on the central host with no context — so the cross-tenant read returned **nothing**, with no error, under an empty state reading "No feedback yet". The test half is the same lesson inverted: `FeedbackRlsTest` had to drop that scope too, because leaving it on makes the RLS assertions pass whether the Postgres policies exist or not. Full detail, plus the four other traps (no route-model binding on console routes; SELECT-only bypass; no `attachments` bypass at all; the PLURAL audit alias), in the **I7a (do not re-open)** block below — **every future console surface over a tenant-scoped table inherits the first two.**

  **⚠️ AND CI CAUGHT A SECOND ONE THE LOCAL TREE COULD NOT — FOR THE SECOND INCREMENT RUNNING.** The first CI run came back RED on **nine unrelated tests** (`DemoSeederIdempotencyTest`, `DatabaseSeederSmokeTest`, and the three sweep-command suites) while every feedback test was green both locally and in CI. Cause: the two console suites seed **committed** rows on `pgsql_privileged` — they must, because the elevated connection cannot see RefreshDatabase's transaction — and followed `SuperAdminBypassTest` in deliberately not cleaning up. **That precedent's reasoning held only because it commits `users` and nothing else.** These commit **TENANTS**, which are global state other suites legitimately assert over. Fixed by `purgeCommittedFeedbackFixtures()` (tests/Pest.php) registered through `beforeApplicationDestroyed()` — which Laravel runs in registration order AFTER RefreshDatabase's own rollback, so the DELETE lands once the transaction is gone and the deadlock the old note feared cannot happen. **The lesson generalises past this increment: a committed cross-connection fixture is global state, and `migrate:fresh wipes it next run` is not a cleanup strategy inside a single suite run.**

  **Also found:** `token-references.test.ts` — the suite this file once dismissed as a known flake — caught a genuinely undefined `--mds-color-border-subtle`, for the second increment running. And the full local Pest suite now dies at **exactly 981 tests** on the container's **128M** ceiling (no php.ini is loaded at all) while CI runs unlimited: a config divergence, not a code defect. See the Verification loop for the corrected invocation.

- **I6 (as-built, MERGED into `phase1-completion` via PR #105 `4364fbe`, 6/6 CI GREEN — and green again on the post-merge integration state, which is what finally retired I5's verification debt) — the DEMO SEED, the LANDINGS, and `docs/TESTING-GUIDE.md`: the increment that makes the app testable by hand.** Three deliverables that only make sense together, plus one live bug found on the way in.

  **⚠️ `DatabaseSeeder` WAS BROKEN, AND HAD BEEN FOR A LONG TIME.** It carried `WithoutModelEvents`, which wraps its whole `run()` — **and every seeder it `->call()`s** — in `Model::withoutEvents()`. That suppresses `HasUuidv7::creating` (which fills the uuid PK; `users.id` has **no database default**) and `BelongsToTenant::creating` (which fills `tenant_id`, without which strict RLS refuses the row). So `migrate:fresh --seed` — the first command the testing guide asks a human to run — raised 23502. Nothing caught it because **nothing has ever executed that file**: `ci.yml` seeds only `E2eSeeder`, and no test referenced `DatabaseSeeder`. Its two ad-hoc `User::factory()->create()` calls were also un-upserted, so a second `db:seed` 23505'd on the email. Both are gone, the trait is gone, and **`DatabaseSeederSmokeTest` now runs the file** — the single highest-value test in this increment.

  **`DemoSeeder` NEVER TOUCHES `acme`, AND THAT IS A CONSTRAINT RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE.** `E2eSeederIdempotencyTest` pins that fixture's counts exactly (7 published, 33 submissions, 7 notifications, Owner unread 4); writing one row into it would couple a convenience fixture to a merge-blocking gate. Two tenants of its own instead: **`demo`** (Business, the walkthrough) and **`northwind`** (Starter, deliberately thin), all five roles plus a **cross-tenant consultant** and a pending invite, six forms with distinct jobs, ~520 submissions over 90 days, and audit/notification/feedback/settings variety. Reachable at `demo.localhost:8080` / `northwind.localhost:8080`; every login is `meridian-demo-2026`.

  **⚠️ SUBMISSIONS ARE HAND-ROLLED, NEVER `SubmissionPipeline`** — the five recorded reasons (`E2eSeeder.php:1339-1348`) all hold, and the first alone settles it: `persist()` hard-codes `submitted_at => now()`, collapsing a 90-day spread into one bucket. Index rows go through **`AnswerIndexProjector`**, the production writer's own collaborator, so the fixture cannot drift from `SubmissionFinalizer::projectIndex()`.

  **⚠️ THE AUDIT TAIL IS BACK-DATED BY INSERT, AND THAT IS LEGAL WHERE AN UPDATE IS NOT.** `audits` is `append_only` RLS: strict SELECT, strict INSERT, **no UPDATE or DELETE policy at all** — so the `forceFill(...)->saveQuietly()` idiom used for notifications is structurally impossible here. Supplying `created_at` on an INSERT is not an UPDATE, and the INSERT policy only constrains `tenant_id`. **No `occurredAt` was added to `AuditLogger`** (`E2eSeeder.php:1096-1099` forbids it, and that still stands). The rows' `redacted_fields` is **PRODUCED by `AuditRedactor`**, `AuditLogger`'s own collaborator, never authored — the same move `AnswerIndexProjector` represents for the index. The reason it is worth doing at all: a human is about to test the audit log's **date filters**, and a ledger where every row landed at `now()` gives them nothing to select over.

  **⚠️ `reviewer` AND `form_editor` LACK `dashboard.org.view`, SO WITHOUT SEEDED RESOURCE GRANTS THEIR INBOX IS EMPTY** and the guide fails on its first review step. They get three forms each — and **deliberately NOT `Patient Intake`**, which carries ~420 of the ~520 rows. Granting it made the contrast 446-vs-497: correct, and invisible. Excluding it makes it **104 vs 497**, which is the whole point of showing a permission model to a human. Found by walking the guide over HTTP, not by reading the code.

  **⚠️ `resolveOrCreateUser()` MEMOISES PER RUN, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING.** `consultant@demo.test` belongs to both tenants, so it is resolved twice in one `run()`. Outside a transaction the first `create()` has committed and the second lookup finds it; under `RefreshDatabase` the whole run sits in ONE uncommitted transaction on the default connection, which `pgsql_auth` cannot see — so without the memo the second resolve takes the create arm and 23505s, **in tests only, for a seeder that works perfectly from the CLI**.

  **`$historyDays` LETS TESTS SEED A SHORT RUN**, which took the seeder suite from 340s to 52s. Sound only because every derived value is a function of the row **index** and never the row **count**, so a short spec is a genuine PREFIX of a long one — pinned directly by `it('derives every submission row from its index')`, with one full-scale test still asserting the shipped numbers.

  **THE LANDINGS ARE ONE ROUTE, AND THE TRACKER'S OWN PRESCRIPTION FOR THIS ROW WOULD HAVE BROKEN A PINNED TEST.** The load-order half was right — `routes/web.php` registers ~70 slots before anything in `tenant.php` (mapped in `app->booted()`), so a tenant `/` there is dead code. But adding `Route::domain(central_domain)` to free it up then lets the tenant route match **custom** hosts, where `InitializeTenancyBySubdomain` throws → **302**, exactly where `CustomDomainRoutingTest:158-167` pins a **404**. It would also diverge `localhost` from `127.0.0.1` locally, since both are `central_domains` but only one is `central_domain`. `RequirePlatformHost` already draws the right line, so `PlatformLandingController` **branches inside the existing route**: central host → the landing page, workspace host → `/dashboard` or `/login` by auth state, unknown label → the central landing, custom domain → still 404. `PlatformLandingTest` asserts "404, **and specifically not a redirect**" so a later attempt fails loudly. **`$request->user()` is safe there with no tenant GUC** — the `users` provider is `rls_aware` and resolves on `pgsql_auth`, which is why login already works on those same middleware-less Fortify routes. The closure became a controller because a route **closure makes `route:cache` throw**.

  **`Welcome.vue` was a 37-line walking-skeleton stub with hard-coded `#64748b`**; it is now a real landing page built entirely from tokens, and the first surface to consume `--mds-type-display` / `--mds-space-16` / `--mds-space-24`, which the DSR has reserved for exactly this class of page since G11. Logged as **exceptions-log #8** (the log already ran to #7 — an earlier draft said #5). Its `registrationOpen` prop means the "Create a workspace" button is **absent** rather than leading to a `GateRegistration` 404 when platform signup is off. It joins `responsive-axe` as the **first central-domain page in that matrix**, which is legitimate: the standing exclusion is about `superadmin.mfa` needing a TOTP, and `/` needs no auth at all.

  **THE GUIDE WAS WRITTEN AGAINST THE RUNNING APP, AND THAT CAUGHT FOUR ERRORS BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** There is **no bare `GET /admin` route** (the console's entry is `/admin/tenants`); the settings cards are Profile/Appearance/Notifications/Drafts/Access/Maintenance/Modules/Custom domains/Branding/Security/About; **Pest does NOT share the dev database** (`phpunit.xml` pins `meridian_testing`, so only Playwright can disturb the demo data — the blanket warning was wrong); and drafts are hidden from the unfiltered inbox but **do** appear under an explicit Draft filter. Chapter 17 is the deliberate other half: a table of what is **not** built, each row naming the increment that owns it, so a missing feature reads as a known gap rather than a bug.

  **Registered by citation, not by number** — the `#1–#27` index belongs to the architecture plan's §4 list, which this repo does not own, so a `#28` would be claiming someone else's slot. Linked from `README.md` and cited in `docs/PRD.md:13`, exactly as `docs/feature-backlog.md` is.

  **Local gates, all green on the merged tree:** Pest **2757/2757** (1007s, run ALONE — **+17 = exactly the tests this increment adds**), PHPStan **55 = baseline exactly**, Pint clean, migration-lint + controller-gate + job-payload-lint pass, `composer audit` + `npm audit --omit=dev` clean, Vitest **1307/1307 across 67 files**, vue-tsc clean, `npm run build` clean, `openapi.json` regenerated and **byte-identical** (web-only routes; Scramble scopes to `api/v1`).

  **⚠️ CI'S FIRST REAL RUN ON THIS BRANCH FOUND TWO FAILURES, NEITHER OF THEM I6'S, AND THAT IS THE MOST USEFUL THING THIS INCREMENT PRODUCED.** Every local gate was green — Pest 2757/2757, clean audits — and CI still came back red once the outage lifted and jobs could actually execute. **(1)** Two `TenantMaintenanceTest` cases were missing `$this->withoutVite()`: the only two in that file that render the real guest shell (`public-runtime.blade.php` carries `@vite`) and expect a 200, where the three paused cases render the maintenance blade and were therefore green. The Pest job builds no assets, so `@vite` throws and the 200 arrives as a **500**; locally they passed only because `public/build` happened to exist. **They are I5 defects, and CI had never once been allowed to run them** — I5's Pest job was cancelled by the outage on all four attempts. Reproduced locally by moving `public/build/manifest.json` aside, per this file's own instruction: 7/7 with the fix. **(2)** Three `league/commonmark` advisories — including **CVE-2026-71488**, a high-severity quadratic-time DoS — were published 2026-08-06 20:30–20:39 UTC, i.e. **after** this session's clean local `composer audit` and **before** CI ran its own. Transitive, so `composer.json` is untouched; 2.8.2 → 2.9.0, two packages move in the lockfile, 327 mail/notification/branding tests pass on the new version. **The standing lesson: an unverified gate is not a green gate, and a clean local audit has a shelf life measured in hours.**

- **I5 (as-built, MERGED into `phase1-completion` via PR #104 `50849fb` — ⚠️ 2/6 CI CONFIRMED, see "OUTSTANDING VERIFICATION" below; E2E 22m40s PASS + a11y 3m53s PASS, the other four never acquired a runner during a GitHub Actions MAJOR OUTAGE) — APP SETTINGS (PRD Feature #10) + the design system's first `MdsSwitch`.** Closes the second of the three Phase-1 features the 08-06 audit found unbuilt. Every local gate was run to completion on the merged tree: **Pest 2740/2740** (982s, run ALONE — see the trap below), PHPStan **55 = baseline exactly**, Pint clean, `composer audit` + `npm audit --omit=dev` clean, vue-tsc clean, `npm run build` clean, **Vitest 1292/1292 across 67 files**, `openapi.json` regenerated and **byte-identical**, migration-lint + controller-gate pass.

  **⚠️ THE STORE IS SPLIT ACROSS TWO TABLES ON PURPOSE, AND THE RULE IS *WHERE IT IS READ*, NOT WHERE IT IS WRITTEN.** `settings` rows hold `registration.invite_only` and `modules.*`; **tenant maintenance mode is two REAL COLUMNS on `tenants`** (`maintenance_mode`, `maintenance_message`) because the guest runtime consults it on **every public form render**, where stancl has already resolved and bound the tenant — a row would put an extra query in front of every respondent forever, and caching buys nothing (`CACHE_STORE=database` ⇒ a cache read is the same round trip). Third instance of the `draft_ttl_days`/branding rule, not an exception; data-dictionary §20 + §1 amended in-PR. Both columns are in `getCustomColumns()` — **omit that and maintenance mode saves, reads back null, and never applies, fail-OPEN, with a green write path** (`TenantMaintenanceColumnsTest` pins the list AND a real round trip).

  **⚠️ TWO PARTIAL UNIQUE INDEXES, NOT ONE `UNIQUE(tenant_id, key)`** — Postgres treats NULL as distinct from NULL, so a plain constraint polices the tenant rows and silently permits **two platform rows for one key**. `SettingsRlsTest` asserts each index bites with its own 23505.

  **⚠️ A PLATFORM ROW IS UNWRITABLE FROM ANY TENANT CONNECTION, AND IT FAILS SILENTLY** — nullable_global widens SELECT only, so a naive `update()` affects **zero rows and raises nothing**. All platform writes go through `SuperAdminService::updatePlatformSettings()` on `pgsql_superadmin` via `applySuperAdminBypass('settings', ['INSERT','UPDATE'])` **plus** an explicit GRANT (table privileges are checked BEFORE RLS, so the policy alone is not enough — the test asserts grant and policy SEPARATELY, or a missing policy passes on the strength of the grant).

  **`applySuperAdminBypass('audits', ['INSERT'])` is the deferred half of a decision H4 already took in writing** (`create_audits_table.php`: *"would only be needed for a future NULL-tenant / cross-tenant PLATFORM audit action… deferred to that action"*). I5 is that action. **INSERT only — the ledger stays immutable and no UPDATE/DELETE policy may ever be added to `audits`.** The NULL-tenant rows are invisible to every tenant `/audit-log` by construction; that is the intent, not a gap (audit spec §1). **PINNED AND HONOURED: tenant settings audits stay on `auditable_type='settings'` / `auditable_id = tenants.id`** — I2's rule — and `TenantAppSettingsTest` asserts the tenant id positively AND denies the settings-row uuid.

  **Module toggles are a THIRD layer in `EntitlementService::feature()`: effective = (legacy ∥ plan) AND NOT tenant-disabled.** PRD #10 demands the existing capability-flag mechanism, "not a second flagging system" — so every `feature:<key>` route gate and the shared `entitlements` prop honour it with no other change (`snapshot()` calls `feature()` per key). **One-directional by construction**: the toggle is tenant-writable and the plan flag is not, so winning outright would make an Owner's own settings row an escalation path. The offered set is CURATED (`ToggleableModules::KEYS`) — `branding`/`custom_domain` excluded because switching either off hides the surface ADR-0012 §D9's downgrade escape hatch needs while a hostname still resolves.

  **THE SELF-JOIN IS REAL, AND THE TRAP THAT NEARLY KILLED IT IS WORTH REMEMBERING.** Before I5 the Access toggle's "open" position could do nothing — `/register` created an account belonging to **no workspace at all**. `TenantMembershipService::joinOpenTenant()` now mints the membership at **viewer**, audited `via: self_registration`. ⚠️ **Fortify's routes carry NO tenancy middleware**, so `/register` has no tenant GUC — and under nullable_global's SELECT that makes a tenant's own rows **INVISIBLE, not absent**, so a naive read falls back to the invite-only default and "open" is unreachable **forever, failing CLOSED and silently**. `TenantSettingRegistry::forTenant()` borrows the context inside a `DB::transaction` (**`applyLocal()` is `SET LOCAL` — a no-op outside one**). `GateRegistration` refuses with **404, never 403** (a 403 confirms both that the workspace exists and that it is invite-only), and it **checks `$request->is('register')` FIRST** because `config/fortify.php`'s middleware list applies to *every* Fortify route — without that guard, closing signup would 404 `/login` for everyone.

  **⚠️ `EnforcePlatformMaintenance` IS GLOBAL AND EXEMPTS BY PATH, NEVER BY ROUTE NAME** — global middleware runs BEFORE routing, so `$request->route()` is null and a name-based list exempts NOTHING, starting with `/admin/*`: the operator would be locked out of the only page that switches maintenance off. **`EnforceTenantMaintenance` must sort AFTER identification** or there is no bound tenant, everything passes through, and the feature is **dead with a green build** — `TenancyMiddlewarePriorityTest` gained that assertion and it was **MUTATION-VERIFIED** (moving the class above `InitializeTenancyByPublicHost` reddens it). One `MaintenanceResponse` factory decides the shape once: 409 + `X-Inertia-Location` for an Inertia XHR (a bare 503 renders as Inertia's debug modal *on top of* the app), the §2.3 envelope on `/api/v1`, JSON, else the 503 blade. **Return, never `abort()`** — a throw is re-shaped by `bootstrap/app.php`'s `Throwable` fallback into `server_error`.

  **`MdsSwitch` is a real `<input type="checkbox">` with `role="switch"`, prop-for-prop identical to `MdsCheckbox`** — which is *why* the 15-control swap was mechanical, and **`NotificationPreferencesCard.test.ts` passing BYTE-UNCHANGED across it is the proof, not a coincidence**. Thumb position is the non-colour signifier (WCAG 1.4.1); DSR §3.2's "specified but NOT built" callout is rewritten with that argument per §1.2's same-PR rule. `BrandingCard`'s header was unstyled since H23a2 (scoped CSS reaches a child SFC's ROOT NODE only, and it was the one card missing the re-declared block) — fixed.

  **NEW LOCAL-GATE LESSON, PAID FOR TWICE IN ONE SESSION:** running a second Pest process while the full suite is in flight `migrate:fresh`es the schema underneath it. It surfaced as three `users_email_unique` violations — one of them in the pre-existing `MembershipRoutesTest`, which is what made the cause unambiguous. All three passed in isolation and the suite was **2740/2740 when run alone**. The tracker already warned about this; treat it as a hard rule, not a caution.

  **DEFERRED, WITH AN OWNER:** the admin console stays out of Playwright (`playwright.config.ts`'s header records why — `superadmin.mfa` needs a TOTP in CI); `docs/feature-backlog.md` §7 gained the row, owner **I8**, which already owns the same `globalSetup`/`storageState` restructure.

- **I4 (as-built, MERGED via PR #103 `ec97ae1`, 6/6 CI GREEN ON THE FIRST RUN — Contract 53s, frontend 1m12s, static 1m58s, a11y 2m21s, Pest 3m48s, E2E 14m41s) — the NOTIFICATIONS UI (PRD Feature #13b): the bell finally shows what I3 has been writing.** Popover-only centre (no `/notifications` page — PRD #13 asks for "recent events", and a full list is a second surface with no second job), a per-type preferences card on `/settings`, and no new permission key, no `feature:` gate and **no `openapi.json` change** (web-only routes; `config/scramble.php` scopes generation to `api/v1`). Net-new: `NotificationController` (3 methods), `NotificationPolicy` (one `markRead` ability), `NotificationPresenter`, `UpdateNotificationPreferenceRequest`, `NotificationCopy::inApp()`, `NotificationPreferenceResolver::resolveAll()`, `resources/js/components/notifications/{types,type-visual,relative-time,notificationsClient}.ts`, `useNotificationFeed.ts`, a rewritten `NotificationBell.vue`, `NotificationPreferencesCard.vue`, `E2eSeeder::seedNotifications()`, four Pest suites + a Unit suite + a seeder-convergence case, six Vitest suites and a `responsive-axe` interaction block. **THREE DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED THAT WERE NOT ON THE PLAN, none of them findable from the happy path:** (1) `GeneratePdfJob` records an `export_ready` row for **every** terminal outcome, so a quota-blocked or failed export produced a bell row headed "Export ready" over a submission with no artifact on it — the email arm was always honest, the bell was not; (2) `SubmissionPolicy::view()` has **no respondent clause**, yet `submission_approved`/`submission_returned` are addressed to `respondent_user_id`, so a form editor whose grant is later revoked keeps a notification pointing at Laravel's **bare 403 page, outside the Inertia shell, with no navigation back** — I4 makes that honest rather than broken (see D5 below) and files the authorization question for I9; (3) the first spelling of the failed-export title was `!== Ready`, which renders a MISSING outcome as a failure and contradicts the sentence underneath it — caught by its own test, before any of it shipped. Gates: Pest **2595** (+77), Vitest **1284** (+66 across six new files), PHPStan **55/0 new**, Pint, controller-gate, migration-lint, job-payload-lint, `composer audit` + `npm audit --omit=dev` clean, `openapi.json` byte-identical.

- **H16a (as-built, MERGED into `phase1-completion` via PR #99 `1f38296`, 6/6 CI GREEN — Contract 46s, frontend 1m21s, static 1m58s, a11y 2m11s, Pest 4m2s, E2E 13m22s) — the Google Sheets connector backend, and the SHARED column-mapping/drift engine that is the reason this row was chosen.** H-map row 240 (`backend+api, L`, depends H15a + H6a — both merged), un-deferred from Phase 3b by user decision 2026-08-05 after the four options were put to them. **The reason is structural, not preference: H19a depends on H18b AND H16a**, so this clears H19a's second, independent blocker while the OCR chain stays blocked on documents. Gate `native_connectors` Starter+, INHERITED — no new gate, no new ability, no new `UsageMetric`, no new `QueueName`. **FOUR DECISIONS LOCKED WITH THE USER UP FRONT:** (1) a row carries **MAPPED ANSWERS**, not the metadata envelope; (2) scopes are **per-file, never all-Drive**; (3) **pre-flight refresh + corrected classification**; (4) drift **pauses the rule and notifies the owner**. **THE SHARED ENGINE IS `App\Support\Mapping\*`** — `ColumnFingerprint` / `ColumnMapping` / `MappedColumn` / `MappingDrift` / `MappingDriftDetector`, pure (no HTTP, no Eloquent, no container) and pinned by `MappingNamespaceBoundaryTest`, which PARSES the namespace's own source and fails if it imports from Connectors/Ocr/Database/Http. **BOTH DIRECTIONS SHIP NOW** — `project()` writes (H16a) and `interpret()` reads (H19a's linelist) — so the reuse is demonstrable rather than asserted; a one-directional engine is exactly how H19a would have discovered the shape did not fit and written a second one. **ORDER IS PART OF THE FINGERPRINT** because both consumers address columns positionally; a set-based digest would call a reordered sheet identical and put every answer under the wrong heading, which is the failure `ocr-pipeline-design.md:84` names. **VERIFICATION NARROWED A LOCKED DECISION, IN THE DIRECTION THE USER CHOSE:** the plan said add `spreadsheets` only if required — Google's own scope table classifies `drive.file` **Recommended, Non-sensitive** for the Sheets API and `spreadsheets` **Sensitive**, so `drive.file` ships ALONE, avoiding both the wider grant and Google's sensitive-scope verification tier. **THE SHARPEST FACT IN THE INCREMENT: Google answers HTTP 401 `authError` for an EXPIRED access token and a REVOKED grant alike.** Only the token endpoint distinguishes them (`invalid_grant`), so the adapter classifies 401 as **retryable**; the obvious mapping would have revoked a live connection roughly once an hour, clearing both tokens and emailing the owner each time. **FOUR NET-NEW FRAMEWORK CHANGES, each because the alternative was wrong:** a **fourth `ConnectorDeliveryResult` outcome `blocked`** (destination unusable, credential healthy → pause ONE rule; `failed` would retry a human-only fix and `credentialRejected` would revoke a good grant over one added column); **`ConnectionTokenRefresher::ensureFresh()`** (ADR-0009 §D6's own revisit trigger, fired — Google's ~1h tokens vs an hourly sweep — as a 120s GUARD on top of the sweep, not a second policy); a **`$terminal` flag on `ConnectorOAuthException`** (the refresher marked a grant permanently dead on ANY exception **including a transport timeout** — unreachable in H15a because Slack tokens never expire, reachable on every Google send from here, and found because PHPStan reported the adapter's CREDENTIAL_ERRORS constant as unused); and **`SubscriptionConfigRules`**, because H15a's four request classes hard-coded Slack's `config.channel_id` in the shared layer, so a Sheets rule was rejected by validation before any adapter saw it. **Two more Slack-shaped seams in provider-agnostic code fixed:** `ConnectorChannelDirectory::message()` said "Slack" in every string, and `ConnectionPresenter::providerDescription()` is a `default`-less `match` that would have thrown `UnhandledMatchError` on the Integrations page. **`valueInputOption=RAW` IS A SECURITY CONTROL, not formatting:** `security-threat-model.md:127`'s formula-injection row in a worse form — a live Sheet evaluates `=IMPORTXML(...)` on GOOGLE'S servers with no human opening anything — and RAW is structural where the CSV path still owes a `'` prefix (that row stays OPEN). `insertDataOption=INSERT_ROWS` because the API DEFAULTS to OVERWRITE, which would destroy whatever the tenant keeps below their table. **`SubmissionRowProjector` EXTRACTED from `SubmissionExporter`** so a synced row and a downloaded row cannot disagree — the seam is a `fieldKey ⇒ value` MAP, not a positional row, because the two consumers order columns differently and neither ordering is more correct. **13 MUTATIONS RUN; ONE CAME BACK GREEN AND INDICTED THE CODE:** the `! $alreadyPaused` edge-trigger was unreachable (the job's own Active-status guard already stops every queued delivery behind the first block), so the flag and its comment are GONE and the test now drives TWO deliveries to pin the real mechanism. **TWO PROCESS LESSONS, both about mutations that lie.** A `sed` whose pattern never matched (`\x1E` in a BRE reduces to `x1E`) and a mutation that introduced a SYNTAX ERROR both reported "green"/"red" for reasons unrelated to the code — **verify a mutation actually applied, and `php -l` it, before believing either colour**. A third mutation was behaviourally EQUIVALENT (`$valuesByFieldKey[null]` coerces to `['']`), which looked like a test gap and was not. **Also: `git checkout --` after a mutation reverts to the COMMITTED state, silently undoing uncommitted work in the same file.** **TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY RE-READING THE ADAPTER AFTER THE SUITE WAS ALREADY GREEN, not by a failing test:** (1) **a sheet name went into A1 notation UNQUOTED** — and Google's own default tab for a form-linked sheet is `Form Responses 1`, with spaces, so `Form Responses 1!1:1` is unparseable and an entirely ordinary destination would have failed; worse, it fails as a **400**, which the classifier treated as transient, so it would have burned the whole seven-day ladder and dead-lettered with the tenant never told why. Quoting is now unconditional, with an internal apostrophe DOUBLED per A1's own rule. (2) **a 400 `INVALID_ARGUMENT` is also Google's answer for a TAB THAT NO LONGER EXISTS** — the likeliest way a working rule breaks after setup — so it is now `blocked` rather than retried. Both are pinned (reverting the quoting reddens 2, reverting the 400 arm reddens 1). **AND THE `git checkout --` TRAP BIT A THIRD TIME IN ONE SESSION**, discarding both of these while they were still uncommitted and making one mutation run measure unfixed code — **commit BEFORE mutating, every time**. **KNOWN NARROWINGS, recorded not discovered later:** no identity scope ⇒ `external_account_id` is a constant ⇒ **one live Google connection per tenant** (a second replaces the first); **no `channel_lister`** (per-file access cannot enumerate — the picker is H16b's client-side Google Picker); **event-driven append only** (scheduled sync + historical backfill are named out of scope); and the exported `openapi.json` shows every destination key as optional, because which are required depends on the connection's provider — **and that contract nearly regressed silently**: Scramble reads `rules()` statically, so returning the whole shape from a method degraded `config` to an array-of-strings, which the CI gate could NOT catch because it only diffs a fresh export against the committed file and both were equally wrong. Fixed with a static `documentedShape()` union plus dynamic `requiredFor()`. **Docs: ADR-0009 §D6 + §D8 amended in place with the reasoning (both revisit triggers fired) and its "When to Revisit" first bullet answered; `data-privacy-gdpr-compliance.md` §7 revised IN THIS INCREMENT as that bullet itself demanded — the connector envelope is no longer metadata-by-default, and the erasure asymmetry (a row already in the tenant's Drive cannot be recalled) is recorded; `webhook-integration-design.md` §4 row 4 built-note; threat-model addendum; data-dictionary §25 `config` shape.**

Reseed an emptied dev DB with `php artisan db:seed --class=E2eSeeder` (idempotent; creates `acme` on the BUSINESS plan, 12 forms, 33 submissions and five guest-enabled slugs — `/f/clinic-intake`, `/f/household-roster`, `/f/field-types`, `/f/closed-survey`, `/f/branching-router`); bare `localhost:8080` = central host.

- **H25 (as-built, MERGED PR #93 `c2e0223`, 6/6 CI green on the FIRST run — Contract 48s, frontend 1m15s, static 1m41s, a11y 2m11s, Pest 3m53s, E2E 11m17s) — the R5 published-version immutability guard, and the schema's FIRST database trigger.** Ships **ADR-0013 in-PR** (the H22a/ADR-0012 precedent). **R5 had been open since the architecture doc was written** and was one of only two rows in the §9 register still marked *Still OPEN*; it lost its forcing moment when the 2026-07-21 decision chose relevance-derived step-skipping over a stored step graph, and became this hardening PR. **THE GAP, PRECISELY:** a published version's CONTENT has been frozen in Postgres since Increment D (`draftChildGuard` gates every child write behind `EXISTS (fv.status = 'draft')`), but its OWN ROW was not — `formVersionGuard` leaves UPDATE deliberately status-blind so publish can flip draft→published→superseded, so `schema_snapshot`, `checksum`, `version_number`, `title`, `published_at` were all freely rewritable on a live published row, **and `status` could be set back to `'draft'`, which RE-OPENS every child row, because the child guard keys on exactly that value**. **RLS structurally cannot close it and this is not a preference:** a policy sees only OLD in `USING` and only NEW in `WITH CHECK`, and no clause can compare them, while a per-column immutability rule IS that comparison; a CHECK cannot see OLD either. **AS BUILT:** `form_versions_published_immutable_trg`, `BEFORE UPDATE … FOR EACH ROW`, gated `WHEN (OLD.status IS DISTINCT FROM 'draft')` — the gate keys on `OLD.status` and NEVER on `published_at`, because `ConnectorFanOutTest:146` legitimately writes `published_at` onto a draft row, and it lives in the `WHEN` clause and nowhere else (two copies drift, and the drift direction where WHEN fires but the body early-returns is fail-open — plus `pg_get_triggerdef()` then carries the predicate, which is what the structural test pins). **(1) DENY BY DEFAULT is the whole design** — there is NO list of frozen columns, because a hand-list is fail-OPEN for the future (a column added later would be silently mutable and nothing in CI would notice: `scripts/migration-lint.php` early-returns on alter-only migrations, so this migration's own green is VACUOUS and the three test files are the only things standing over the guard). It diffs the WHOLE ROW, `to_jsonb(OLD)` vs `to_jsonb(NEW)` per key, against a four-name allowlist — a feature test adds a column inside the test transaction and proves it is already frozen. **(2) Compared as `::text`, not as jsonb** — jsonb equality is numeric-aware (`'1'::jsonb = '1.0'::jsonb` is TRUE), so a scale-only rewrite would pass a jsonb `=` guard while silently invalidating `checksum`, which is SHA-256 over the canonical TEXT and is the offline-sync cache-buster; `::text` still treats a key-reordered snapshot as unchanged (jsonb normalises key order at parse time), and both halves are tested. The projection is also NULL-safe by construction (a SQL NULL becomes the jsonb value `null`), which matters because SEVEN frozen columns are nullable — including `created_at`, since `timestampsTz()` emits both timestamps nullable — and the suite routinely makes published rows with a NULL `checksum`; **no bare `<>` appears anywhere and a unit test asserts that absence**. **(3) FOUR INDEPENDENT RULES, not chained** (a bare `updated_at` touch on a superseded row must pass, or the guard is stricter than the invariant): frozen columns; `status` may only move `published → superseded`, `superseded` terminal, **never back to draft**; `superseded_at` writable only by that flip and only out of NULL; and **`published_by` may be CLEARED but never re-pointed**. **THAT FOURTH RULE IS THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED THIS BROKEN and it is not obvious:** `published_by` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` against `users`, and PostgreSQL runs that referential action as an ordinary UPDATE through SPI — **it bypasses RLS but NOT a trigger** — so freezing the column outright would have made user hard-deletion (and GDPR erasure with it) permanently impossible, raising a form-version error from an unrelated tenant's row on a statement about `users`. A feature test hard-deletes a publisher and asserts the version survives with a null attribution (the publisher must be a distinct user from the form owner — `forms.owner_user_id` is `ON DELETE NO ACTION` — and an active member, because `users` carries a join-shape SELECT policy and Postgres applies SELECT policies to a WHERE-qualified DELETE, so a non-member would match zero rows and the test would pass vacuously). **(4) Refusals raise SQLSTATE 23001** (`restrict_violation`) naming every offending column (`string_agg`, alphabetical), the row id and the status — unambiguous here because Postgres core never emits it (a RESTRICT FK raises 23503) and nothing else in the codebase does, which is also what proves the TRIGGER refused a write and not the policy (an RLS `WITH CHECK` failure is 42501, and BEFORE ROW triggers run first). **Deliberately NO renderer in `bootstrap/app.php`:** reaching this guard means a code path bypassing the service layer, and a loud 500 is correct — mapping it to a friendly error would normalise exactly what R5 exists to surface. **SCOPE IS UPDATE ONLY, AND THE RESIDUAL IS NEW RISK R12** (user decision, taken before building): a `BEFORE DELETE` row trigger fires on FK cascades too, so it would turn tenant hard-delete and form hard-delete into an error, and would break `SavedReportViewWebTest:239`. So a `forms`/`tenants` hard-delete still wipes published versions through the cascade (referential actions bypass RLS) and `TRUNCATE` bypasses row triggers entirely — recorded as **R12** with a revisit trigger (the first production hard-delete path) rather than silently inherited. **The guarantee is worded "a published version cannot be EDITED", never "cannot be destroyed", everywhere it appears.** **A COMPANION `form_versions_status_chk` SHIPS IN THE SAME MIGRATION and is not scope creep** — `status` was a bare `varchar(20)` with no CHECK, and the trigger TURNS THAT INTO A TRAP by treating anything that is not `'draft'` as frozen, so one typo'd status would freeze a row permanently in a state no code can read. **`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` IS MANDATORY, NOT STYLISTIC, AND CI CANNOT CATCH IT:** `db:wipe` drops tables/views/types and NEVER routines, so `DROP TABLE … CASCADE` takes the trigger while the function survives — a bare `CREATE FUNCTION` dies on the SECOND `migrate:fresh` on any database, which every CI job avoids by provisioning a fresh one, so it would ship green and break only developers. Verified by running `migrate:fresh` twice. `down()` drops trigger-then-function (the trigger DEPENDS on the function) and never `CASCADE`s. **NAMING — this PR names the convention**, since the repo had zero triggers, trigger functions, `DB::unprepared` or `plpgsql` anywhere: **`_trg` for a trigger, `_fn` for its function, same base name, the table prefixing the function** (function names are schema-global, trigger names are per-table), all under the 63-byte `NAMEDATALEN` that truncates SILENTLY. **THREAT MODEL, STATED HONESTLY RATHER THAN OVERCLAIMED:** `meridian_app` OWNS the table, so it can `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER`; a superuser can `SET session_replication_role = 'replica'`. That is the asymmetry with RLS, where `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` was chosen *precisely because* it binds the owner — so this is an **integrity guard against application bugs and raw queries** (R5's own wording is "accidentally mutates"), **not a security boundary**. What it DOES add over RLS: it binds `pgsql_privileged` (a real superuser bypasses RLS and never a trigger — its own test pack, which needs a COMMITTED fixture on that connection or it passes vacuously, since RefreshDatabase wraps only the default one), and it fires on referential actions. **No GUC hatch** (a custom `app.*` GUC is settable by any role, so it would be no obstacle to the threat); the break-glass is written down instead — `DISABLE TRIGGER` … `ENABLE TRIGGER` inside its own migration, loud and reviewable. Trigger left in origin mode (`tgenabled = 'O'`), not `ENABLE ALWAYS`, which would fire during logical-replication apply and break a future replica restore replaying a supersede. **ONE LIVE DEFECT CLOSED IN PASSING, and it was the one reachable production path into this hole:** `FormService::updateMetadata()` wrote `title`/`description` to `whereKey($form->draft_version_id)` from an IN-MEMORY `$form`, with no lock and no re-read — so if that model was loaded before a concurrent publish, an ordinary form-settings save silently rewrote a PUBLISHED version's title, which is literally the R5 bug; now predicated on `status = 'draft'` so the stale write degrades to a no-op. **ONE DRIFTED FACT CORRECTED:** `FormBuilderService::rowVersion()`'s docblock claimed microsecond precision — Laravel's `Schema\Builder::$defaultTimePrecision` is 0 and the repo never overrides it, so the column is `timestamp(0)`; the real consequence (two builder edits inside one second are an undetectable lost update) is RECORDED, not fixed here, because it is not R5. **DELIBERATELY NOT CLOSED, recorded rather than built:** `draft → superseded` directly (the trigger is silent on draft rows by construction, and firing on them is what the operation-scoping forbids); it is a lifecycle bug, not an immutability breach, because nothing was ever published. **Docs: ADR-0013 + reconciliations to seven siblings** — `form-versioning-schema-migration.md` (**v1.1**, new §2.1: §2's "RLS, not a trigger" resolution STANDS for the three child tables and is narrowed to them, with the boundary stated once — *row-scoped ⇒ RLS, OLD-vs-NEW ⇒ trigger, one member today*), `architecture/technical-architecture.md` (R5 → **RESOLVED**, **new R12**, §6.2 point 4's note amended where it asserted "one single DB-level-guard idiom", Appendix A item 5 closed — and promoted to an ADR exactly as that appendix's closing line anticipated), `data-dictionary.md` §3 (the file had NO mention of any immutability guard), `adr/0002` (a *narrowed, not superseded* note — it is what made "RLS is the one idiom" load-bearing), `workflow-branching-design.md` (§1 and the deferred list both pointed at H25), `testing-strategy.md` (the pack, plus the three reusable harness rules it encodes), `PRD.md` (ADR index). **Gates: Pest 2201 (+52 — 17 unit + 32 feature + 3 privileged), full suite green**; PHPStan **exactly 55**, the documented local phantom baseline, **zero introduced, verified PER FILE** (the two naming `FormBuilderService` are the pre-existing `FormField::$created_by`/`$updated_by` phantoms whose line numbers merely moved 9 down); Pint / controller-gate / migration-lint / job-payload-lint green; **no `openapi.json` change** (DDL-only cannot drift Scramble), no frontend change, golden corpora byte-untouched. The 1400s suite time is the documented host-memory-pressure artifact, not this branch.

- **H23b (as-built, MERGED PR #98 `b3a7971`, 6/6 CI GREEN ON THE FIRST RUN — Contract 50s, frontend 1m28s, static 1m35s, a11y 2m21s, Pest 3m43s, E2E 13m43s) — tenant branding reaches the PUBLIC GUEST RUNTIME, offline-cached; the branding vertical is COMPLETE and Phase 3 has no unblocked rows left.** **The E2E job is the load-bearing gate here**: it is the only one that loads the guest runtime in a real Chromium, registers the service worker, primes the caches and reloads offline — so the shared partial, the corrected `theme-color` and the `?b=` manifest link are verified against a live browser rather than only against assertions. (The local dev DB is EMPTY — 0 tenants, 0 forms — so the manual browser check in the plan was deliberately NOT done rather than seed the user's environment uninvited; CI's E2E covers the same ground.) Built off `main`, NOT off #97, exactly as row 258 scopes it (tokens + offline cache-invalidation; the logo is not in it). **THE APPLICATION HALF IS THE EASY HALF AND IT STILL HAD ONE TRAP.** The guest shell can never carry `data-theme-mode`, so a guest-specific two-block emission naming only `:root` looked obviously right and is WRONG: inside `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` a bare `:root` is (0,1,0) and **loses** to `theme-overrides.css`'s own system-dark block at `:root:not([data-theme-mode='light']):not([data-theme-mode='dark'])` — (0,3,0). So the emission is a **single shared Blade partial** (`resources/views/partials/brand-ramp.blade.php`) included by BOTH the admin root and the guest shell, third block and all; the `[data-theme-mode='dark']` block is inert on the guest shell and kept anyway, because two copies of that specificity reasoning is precisely the drift the ADR is a cautionary tale about. **Callers own WHETHER, the partial owns HOW**: the admin root additionally withholds the ramp when a member has expressed an accent opinion (H23a3's server-side precedence), while the guest shell has **no precedence rule at all** — a respondent has no preferences for the brand to lose to, which is ADR-0014 §D1's two-layers/two-audiences split working rather than an inconsistency to tidy. **ONE READER FOR THREE SURFACES:** `App\Services\Branding\GuestBrandingPresenter` (sibling of `BrandingPresenter`, which serves the settings card and must DISCLOSE what the engine changed — a respondent is told none of that). `TenantBrandingService::sharedRamp()` **is** usable here, unlike in H23a4: the guest group runs `InitializeTenancyByPublicHost`, so `TenantContract` is bound; a queue worker binds nothing. Still read through `isActive()` and never `hasBrandRamp()` — a downgraded tenant keeps its stored ramp and renders unbranded, pinned by its own test. **ONE LIVE DEFECT CLOSED IN PASSING, on exactly this surface, and the user approved it before building:** the shell's `<meta name="theme-color">` and `PwaManifestController::THEME_COLOR` were both hard-coded `#1B5E5E` = `--mds-accent-teal-600` since G8a — but the guest shell has NEVER emitted `data-accent`, so it paints Blueprint, and the browser chrome plus the installed app's splash screen were tinted a colour appearing **nowhere in the form**. `theme-color` now means one thing in both cases — the light `--mds-color-action-primary-bg` — tenant-derived when branded, `--mds-primary-600` (`#1C4B72`) when not. `background_color` stays `--mds-neutral-50`: §D7 keeps the tenant layer off neutrals, and the two sit adjacent in one payload, which is where that rule is easiest to break. **THE OFFLINE HALF IS THE HARD HALF, AND THE DESIGN DECISION IS "RE-PRIME, NEVER PURGE."** The brand rides the shell HTML, which the SW caches `NetworkFirst` under `guest-shell-html` — so the page in front of the respondent self-heals on any successful online load and needs no invalidation. What does not self-heal is every OTHER `/f/…` shell the device cached earlier (a second form, a resume link), stale until its 7-day expiry. **`caches.delete('guest-shell-html')` is the obvious fix and it is the wrong one**: a stale brand is COSMETIC, a purged shell costs a fieldworker offline access to a form they deliberately primed, which is a core PRD promise — a bad trade in any brand's favour. So `lib/brand-cache.ts` REFRESHES: a 12-hex fingerprint (`substr(sha256(json_encode(tokens)),0,12)`, `'none'` unbranded) rides `data-brand-version` on the mount node; after mount the SPA compares it with Dexie `app_state['brand_version']` and, when different **and online**, re-fetches every cached shell except the current URL and `cache.put`s **only on a 200** (writing a 404 over a working offline shell is strictly worse than the stale colour). **Different and OFFLINE ⇒ defer and change NOTHING, including the fingerprint** — advancing it without having refreshed anything is a lie that permanently suppresses the retry, since every later boot would compare equal and skip. That negative is the single most important behaviour in the file and has its own test. The manifest link carries the same fingerprint as `?b=` because the SW never caches that URL and the browser caches it hard; safe for app identity, since the manifest declares an explicit `id` so the URL is not what pins an installed app. `guest-schema` / `guest-shell-assets` untouched — neither carries brand. Dexie goes to **v2** for the new `app_state` store (a new STORE needs a bump; G8c's un-indexed `conflict_code` correctly did not), with a test proving the v1 outbox survives the upgrade — a bump that dropped a respondent's queued work for a cosmetic feature would be the worst possible trade here. **ONE TEST-HARNESS TRAP WORTH CARRYING, AND IT MAKES A CORRECT PRODUCT LOOK BROKEN:** the natural proof that "the fingerprint moves when the brand moves" is to re-brand mid-test and re-render — and it FAILS, because within one Pest test the container keeps the `Tenant` bound by the FIRST request (stancl `Tenancy::initialize()` early-returns when the tenant key is unchanged, `Tenancy.php:43`), so the second render reports the OLD ramp. Asserting the DERIVATION instead is both correct and stronger: the change property then follows from SHA-256 rather than from one sampled pair. **Any test mutating tenant state between two HTTP calls in one Pest test is exposed to this.** **ONE PROXY ASSERTION RETIRED, DELIBERATELY AND WITH THE PIN LEFT SHARPER:** `GuestRuntimeTest` pinned "no personalization on the guest shell" by searching the WHOLE document for `data-theme-mode` — which the shared partial now legitimately NAMES in a selector. That one axis moved to the `<html>` **open tag** (what the invariant was always about); the other three stayed whole-document, and a new case proves the root element is attribute-free **while the brand block is present**, asserted as a user with maximal preferences. **Gates: Pest 2287 (+11), full suite green; Vitest 1189 (+8, 55 files); vue-tsc BOTH tsconfigs, Vite build, Pint, controller-gate / migration-lint / job-payload-lint all clean; PHPStan exactly 55, zero introduced, VERIFIED PER FILE (none of the three touched/new PHP files appears in the breakdown). No migration, no `/api/v1` route, `openapi.json` byte-identical.** Docs: ADR-0014 as-built (the fifth consuming surface, and it CAN resolve `var()` — so §D8's revisit trigger, which asks for a SIXTH, does not fire), `offline-first-sync-design.md` **§4.1 new** (the re-prime rule + the `app_state` store), DSR §2.9 governing rule, `testing-strategy.md` (three generalising lessons). **ADR-0014's two stale Consequences paragraphs were deliberately NOT touched — PR #97 already strikes both through in place, and duplicating that here guarantees a conflict when #97 lands.**

- **H23a4 (as-built, BUILT + 6/6 CI GREEN ON THE FIRST RUN as PR #97 — Contract 45s, frontend 1m30s, static 1m55s, a11y 2m28s, Pest 4m5s, E2E 13m1s — but DELIBERATELY LEFT UNMERGED at the user's request, 2026-08-05; they want to revisit it first, so do NOT merge it unprompted) — tenant branding reaches EMAIL and the PDF, and the QUEUE is the hard part.** ADR-0014 §D8 stores the derived ramp because three of the five consuming surfaces cannot resolve a CSS custom property; until this increment that was an argument about consumers that did not exist. **THE FACT THE WHOLE INCREMENT TURNS ON, AND IT IS INVISIBLE FROM THE CALL SITE:** a queued `Notification` is delivered by the framework's `SendQueuedNotifications`, **NOT** by `TenantAwareJob` — so on the worker `TenantContext::currentTenantId()` is null, and `TenantBrandingService::isActive()` reads its entitlement half from **exactly that static, not from its `$tenant` argument**. Branding resolved inside `toMail()` therefore fails closed and sends EVERY tenant an unbranded email **with a green suite**; `sharedRamp()` is worse still, resolving a container binding no worker makes and returning null unconditionally. So **`App\Support\Branding\BrandPalette` resolves the palette where the tenant IS known and it rides the payload as a scalar array** (R3-legal) — the same discipline every `TenantUrl`-built link already follows. `BrandedMailDispatchTest` **flushes the GUC before running a real worker**, so the test fails if the payload is not already correct. `BrandPalette::forTenant()` additionally **REFUSES TO ANSWER** when its argument is not the ambient tenant, turning `isActive()`'s latent cross-tenant read — plan question for one tenant, storage question for another, no error — into defined, fail-closed behaviour. **THE MAIL LAYER IS "THE FIRST OF ALL OF THOSE", exactly as `TenantMail`'s docblock predicted in H17:** no `resources/views/mail`, no `resources/views/vendor`, no published theme. Three files, one under `vendor/`: `mail/meridian.blade.php` (**Blade, not `.css`** — the view finder maps a `.css` view to the FileEngine and would emit the literal `{{ }}`), `mail/notification.blade.php` (**ours** rather than a published `notifications::email`, because a Blade component does NOT inherit the caller's scope so `$brand` must be injected at the tag), and `vendor/mail/html/header.blade.php` — **the ONLY vendor override, and it is not optional**: the same view renders through an html arm AND a text arm, so an `<img>` in the layout slot is `strip_tags`'d out of the plaintext and one in the stock header's slot is escaped and printed literally. **H23a4 declines `TenantMail` for the FOURTH time** and the prediction is amended in place. **THE LOGO NEEDED A ROUTE THAT DID NOT EXIST:** H23a2's only logo-serving route is `auth` + `can:view,attachment`, which against a mail client is a 302 to login. So `GET /branding/logo`, unauthenticated, on the tenant APP host (ADR-0009 §D2 scopes a custom domain to the guest runtime), **deliberately UNSIGNED** because an expiry on an image sitting in an inbox is a broken image on a timer — bounded instead by resolving `tenants.logo_attachment_id` and never an id from the path (so it is not a public read primitive over `attachments`, and removal takes effect immediately), the `isActive()` gate, the scan-status gate, `nosniff`, and H23a2's content-sniffed allowlist that keeps SVG out of storage. Every negative answers **404**, never 409/403. **THE PDF GETS COLOUR ONLY, AND THAT IS A DECISION:** dompdf needs `ext-gd` for PNG and WebP (JPEG is the only GD-free path) and GD is absent from the app container **AND from all four CI jobs** — a logo would have rendered on a developer's machine and thrown in the pipeline. Three declarations take literal light hexes; the no-external-references contract is untouched and now asserted rather than assumed. **LIGHT THEME ONLY, four independent reasons:** `CssToInlineStyles` deletes `@media` from the theme before inlining; a rule moved into a `<style>` tag loses to the inline declarations the inliner just wrote onto every element; the framework layout hard-codes `color-scheme: light`; and the dark ramp was measured against Meridian's own dark grounds, not whatever ground a client invents when it inverts a message. **ONE LIVE DEFECT CLOSED IN PASSING, on exactly this surface:** `security-threat-model.md` §5 carried "an untrusted value carries markdown syntax into a queued email" as **Open — a live defect, assigned to H3/H23**. Script was already blocked incidentally, but `[` was not, so a tenant named `[Reset your password](https://evil.example)` rendered a **WORKING PHISHING LINK** inside a platform-branded email. `Markdown::withSecuredEncoding()` in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` closes it, **mutation-verified**; residual stated rather than implied (`*`, `_`, backticks stay live — typography, and both named vectors need `[`). **TWO STALE PARAGRAPHS IN ADR-0014 CORRECTED:** H23a3 fixed DSR §395's wrong prediction but left the ADR's own two copies standing — "enforced by CSS specificity" and the `data-accent="blueprint"` restoring rule; both are struck through IN PLACE with the reasoning, because the reasoning is what a future reader would repeat. **The two Fortify emails are deliberately the ONLY unbranded ones** (those routes carry no tenancy middleware, and a user may belong to several tenants, so "whose brand" has no correct answer) — they still render through the Meridian template. **`QueuedMailContractTest` went from THREE of eight notifications to all eight** — written in H3 when three existed, and the five added since were never appended; it also guards `$brand` explicitly, because `job-payload-lint` R3 reads `Class_::getProperties()` and **CANNOT SEE a trait-declared property**. **Gates: Pest 2337 (+61), full suite green; Vitest 1181 unchanged; PHPStan exactly 55, zero introduced, VERIFIED PER FILE (`TenantMembershipService`'s two are pre-existing, line-shifted 7 down by the insertion); Pint / vue-tsc / Vite build / controller-gate / migration-lint / job-payload-lint / `composer audit` clean. No migration, no `/api/v1` route, `openapi.json` byte-identical.** **ONE PROCESS LESSON:** the container's CLI `memory_limit` is exactly **128M** (`php:8.4-fpm-alpine` ships no php.ini), and **`php -d memory_limit=… artisan test` does NOT reach Pest's child process** — a full-suite run needs `php -d memory_limit=2G vendor/bin/pest` directly. Two concurrent Pest runs were also started by accident, which is the documented `migrate:fresh`-under-the-other hazard; both were killed and one clean pass re-run.
