§3's "fixed catalog" refers to the five **roles**) — writing it would have *introduced* an error, so the R6 dangling-pointer fix was substituted as the tenth reconciliation; and *"PRD lists 8 roadmap items"* is unfounded (the 12 is verbatim at `PRD.md:405`; the 8 came from this tracker). **Two extra defects found during verification were folded in** (user-approved): ADR-0002:76's dangling `[D7]` anchor → `[D6]`, and technical-architecture §5 layer 5, which described a "job middleware" that was never built. Ships **docs-only** — no product code, no migration, no `ci.yml` change.
- Phase 2 = **Increment G (G1→G11) — ✅ COMPLETE, all eleven merged.** G1→G6 MERGED; G7 (XLSForm) COMPLETE; G8 (PWA/offline) COMPLETE; G9 COMPLETE (G9a PR #42 + #43, G9b PR #44 `eb6bda7`); G10 COMPLETE (G10a #45, G10b1 #46, G10b2 #47); **G11 MERGED (PR #48 `506ff97`, all 6 CI jobs green)**. With it, **Increment G and Phase 2 closed.**
- **G11 (as-built)** — richer personalization: accent / text-size / dyslexia-friendly font (PRD Feature #9 Phase 2, DSR §2.9, data-dictionary §19). **The starting premise in this tracker was wrong and is corrected below: the columns did NOT exist.** The only migration for `user_ui_preferences` was B1's, which shipped `theme_mode` alone; `accent` was a **hardcoded `'blueprint'` literal** in `User::uiTheme()` that had never touched the database. So G11 *is* a schema increment — new migration `2026_07_21_000002` adds `accent_token varchar(30) NULL` / `font_size_scale varchar(15) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'standard'` / `use_dyslexia_friendly_font boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, matching §19 byte-for-byte, with **no RLS change** (the belongs-to-user policy set is row-level, so it gates rows not columns). **Three new enums** (`ThemeMode`, `FontSizeScale` — both catalogued in §19 since Increment C but never written — plus `AccentToken`, which **is** the §19 application-layer whitelist since that column deliberately has no CHECK). **The keystone finding: the shipped `[data-accent='teal']` block was broken in four ways and nobody could have seen it** — `-bg-hover` pointed at `--mds-primary-700` (a *blue* hover on a teal button), `-bg-active`/`-tint` were never overridden, and there was **no dark variant at all**; since `[data-accent]` carries the same specificity as `[data-theme-mode='dark']` and sat later in source order, teal won in dark and painted light `#1B5E5E` on the `#123350` ground — **1.74:1** for both `action-primary-fg` and the focus ring, failing WCAG 1.4.3 and 1.4.11. It survived two increments because **nothing in the product could set `data-accent` and no test had ever set it**. Fixed by extending `accent-teal` from one step to a **7-step ramp** (50/300/400/500/600/700/800; `600` unchanged, it is ratified) with **all 17 pairings independently computed and verified** — teal is never worse than Blueprint at the equivalent role and is *better* in dark (fill 6.68 vs 6.14, hover 4.82 vs Blueprint's failing 3.96). **The dark+teal payload is defined ONCE** as `--mds-_accent-teal-dark-*` private customs that both the explicit-opt-in selector and the `prefers-color-scheme` twin assign from — the existing neutral dark block is already copy-pasted across those two sites, and a third copy-paste was the obvious future divergence. **The docs' type-scale rule could not ship as written:** §2.9 specified "+2px per level, never changing line-heights", which at extra_large yields `body-sm` as **17px text in an 18px line box (ratio 1.06)** and `caption` 16px-in-16px (1.00) — clipping descenders and failing WCAG 1.4.12, i.e. an accessibility feature shipping an accessibility regression. Replaced (user decision) with **proportional ×1.125/×1.25 scaling of BOTH font-size and line-height**, font-size rounded then line-height **derived** as `round(roundedFontSize × the role's original ratio)` — independent rounding would compress `caption` from 1.333→1.286. Every role now holds within ±2%, floor **1.220**; §2.9 corrected with the authoritative 11×2 table. `code` **is** size-scaled but **not** face-swapped — a deliberate asymmetry (size is a low-vision accommodation and `code` is the smallest role; the face swap is letterform disambiguation for prose, where mono's column alignment would suffer). **OpenDyslexic is vendored** (5.2.5 latin, SIL OFL 1.1, licence verbatim per OFL §2) — the system's first webfont, but **zero-cost by construction**: a browser fetches a `@font-face` src only when a rule using the family matches, and the only such rule is the attribute-gated one, so a user who never opts in downloads **zero font bytes**. The `@font-face` lives in a **new `fonts.css` imported only by the admin app** — `theme-overrides.css` would have shipped it to the guest bundle, since `public-runtime.css` imports that file. **The reflow risk was much smaller than it looked**: `AppLayout`'s `.app-shell__content` is `overflow-y: auto`, which per CSS Overflow makes the other axis `auto` too — so it is its own scroll container and page content never reaches the document's overflow chain that `assertClean` measures. Only `TopNav` was exposed (~344/375px at extra_large, ~31px headroom — one long tenant name from failing), hardened with `min-width: 0` + ellipsis plus an `overflow-x: clip` belt on `.app-shell` (`clip`, not `hidden`: creates no scroll container, does not break `position: sticky`). **Exactly ONE touch to `packages/design-system/src/components/**`, and it is a real bug fix rather than an API change** (see the `MdsDataTable` finding below) — the accent picker is a label-only `MdsSegmentedControl` (a colour swatch would be a colour-ONLY signifier per WCAG 1.4.1, and the control already previews the accent for free since its selected chip is painted with `--mds-color-action-primary-bg`), and the dyslexia toggle reuses `MdsCheckbox` rather than building the specified-but-unbuilt `MdsSwitch` (DSR §3.2 requires a switch carry visible text anyway — i.e. a checkbox with extra steps). That keeps the DSR §1.2 governance gate and the flaky `design-system-a11y` job out of the diff. **`ThemeQuickToggle.vue` diff is literally zero lines**: `useTheme.ts` keeps `useThemePreference()` at its exact C2 signature as a mode-only facade over the new `useAppearancePreference()`. **The anti-clobber guarantee is server-side, not client-side** — every rule on the new `UpdateAppearanceRequest` is `sometimes`, so an absent key is never validated and never reaches `updateOrCreate`; even a stale client cannot wipe an axis it doesn't know about. **Verified by mutation** (making one column write unconditional fails the partial-PATCH test exactly where expected). **Four new guard tests make the hand-authored CSS mechanically verifiable**: `type-scale` re-derives all 44 declarations from the token JSON, `theme-overrides` asserts the duplicated dark blocks are identical (catching the *pre-existing* neutral copy-paste risk too), `token-references` resolves every `var(--mds-*)` in the tree, and `useTheme` pins the "default = ABSENCE of the attribute" convention. **The dead-token sweep found 16 undefined tokens across 10 files, not the 4+ sites PROGRESS estimated** — and it was load-bearing, not cosmetic: `--mds-font-size-body`/`-caption` and `--mds-type-heading-sm-*` had no fallback, so those declarations were invalid at computed-value time and inherited instead, meaning they would have scaled by inheritance rather than by their type role under `data-font-size`. Sites *with* a fallback were collapsed to that fallback (behaviour-preserving) rather than re-pointed, since changing them is a design decision, not a cleanup. **Pest 1001 (+13)**; Pint / controller-gate / migration-lint green; **PHPStan 0 errors on every G11 file**; vue-tsc clean; no `openapi.json` drift (zero API routes — preferences are Inertia web routes). **The E2E found a real bug that no unit test could have, exactly as in G10a — and it was in `MdsDataTable`, not in G11's own code.** At 834px, `extra_large` + the dyslexia face (each harmless alone; only the *combination* triggered it) made the page gain **50px of genuine horizontal scroll** — `window.scrollTo(500,0)` really moved it, so not a `scrollWidth` artifact. Diagnosis mattered more than the fix: `.app-shell`'s `overflow-x: clip` belt made **no difference at all** (identical with `visible`/`hidden`), `body.scrollWidth` was a clean 834 while only `html.scrollWidth` read 884, and every overflowing element was correctly inside the table's `overflow-x: auto` wrapper. The culprit was `.mds-table__sr` — the visually-hidden "Actions" label — which is `position: absolute` while `.mds-table__scroll` was `position: static`, so its containing block resolved **outside** the scroll wrapper and the wrapper could not clip it; a 1px span past the last column then extended the *document's* scrollable width. **No `overflow-x` on any ancestor can fix that**, which is why the belt was useless here: an absolutely positioned element is clipped by its containing block, not by an arbitrary scrolling ancestor. One-line fix (`position: relative` on `.mds-table__scroll`) — a latent DataTable bug that personalization merely made reachable by widening the table, so fixed at source rather than worked around per-page. This is the **only** touch to `src/components/**`, and it satisfies the DSR §1.2 governance gate because the DSR is updated in the same commit. New `personalization-axe` spec is **18 runs, not a 72-run matrix**: extra_large strictly dominates large for reflow and dark+teal is the only genuinely new colour surface, so Loop A scans accent contrast in both themes and Loop B scans only the maximum-stress combination. Narrowings: no UI-interaction E2E (it would persist preferences for the shared storageState user and silently re-tint every later spec under `workers: 1` — persistence is covered by Pest instead); personalization remains authenticated-app-only by design; `MdsSwitch` still unbuilt; Blueprint's own dark `bg-active` measures **2.52:1** with white text — pre-existing, invisible to axe (which never evaluates `:active`), recorded in exceptions-log #6 rather than fixed, to keep the diff to its subject.
- **G10b2 (as-built)** — the scoping hierarchy UI: the surface that finally makes the Reviewer role assignable by a human. **Adds no new authorization logic** — the plan was deliberately shaped to keep that literally true, so the form-scope gate is stacked middleware (`can:update,form` + `can:viewAny,ScopeNode`) rather than a new `FormPolicy::assignScope` method; `ScopeNodePolicy::viewAny` **is** `scopes.manage`, so the two are behaviourally identical and the smaller diff wins. **Transport is forced, not stylistic:** `bootstrap/app.php`'s `$isApi` keys on the `api/v1/*` **path**, and Laravel runs custom render callbacks (`Handler::render():718`) **before** JSON negotiation — so a `ScopeNodeException` on a web route returns `back()->with('toast')`, a 302, **even to an XHR**. `builderClient` would see `response.ok`, skip its guarded branch, and throw a raw `SyntaxError` parsing HTML. Hence: **every mutation is an Inertia visit; the only fetches are two read-only sidecars** (`/scopes/{node}/impact`, `/scopes/{node}/grants`), which raise no domain exception and negotiate 403/404 to JSON cleanly. **`ScopeTree.vue` is the app's first tree widget** (no design-system primitive; there was not one other `role="tree"` in the repo, so every decision here is precedent). Flat `<ul role="tree">` → `<li role="treeitem">` with explicit `aria-level`/`setsize`/`posinset` — valid because `tree.requiredOwned = ['group','treeitem']`, and chosen because visibility, sibling sets and ancestor walks then collapse to one linear pass over the `COLLATE "C"` path order. **The merge-blocker, which neither the design pass nor the judge caught and an adversarial critic did:** `getOwnedRoles` (axe.js:27380) pushes any child carrying a role, a **global `aria-*` attribute**, or focusability as an *owned* child rather than recursing past it, and anything outside `['group','treeitem']` then fails `aria-required-children` (critical, wcag2a) — so the assertive live region and the `MdsEmptyState` are **siblings** of the `<ul>`, and the axe scan targets `.scopes__pane`, not PROGRESS's originally-specified `.scopes__tree`. Roving tabindex runs on a **local `activeId` kept distinct from the server-owned selection**; selection does **not** follow focus (arrow-stepping must not fire the detail panel's sidecar). Row actions live in the detail panel so the grip is the row's only focusable descendant — not because `nested-interactive` would fire (verified against axe source: its matcher keys on `childrenPresentational`, which `treeitem` lacks) but because four buttons per row explodes the tab order and pollutes every row's name-from-contents. **`useScopeTreeMove` is a deliberate SIBLING of `useCanvasReorder`, not a reuse** (that one is typed to `BuilderStore`): it picks a **parent, never a sibling index** — `move()` takes no index, so before/after drop zones are unimplementable — mutates nothing, and confirms, because a move can be refused. No-live-preview is an **a11y** property too: the DOM never reorders mid-grab, so the focused grip cannot be unmounted under the user. **Two real bugs the E2E found:** (1) grab-mode entered from the detail panel's "Move…" focuses the *row*, whose keydown was doing tree navigation — fixed by delegating row keys to the move handler while grabbed; (2) the outcome was announced from the visit's `onSuccess`, but a **no-op or refused** move returns props identical to what is rendered, so a reference watcher never fires — leaving the announcement silent **and the tree stuck in grab mode**. Settled from the refreshed props with an `onFinish` backstop; `settleIfPending` is idempotent so whichever signal lands first wins. **The form→node picker is its own route** (user decision: require `scopes.manage`): writing `scope_node_id` confers capacity on the form **and, through `SubmissionPolicy::collaboratesWith`, its entire submission history** to every holder of a grant on that node and any `includes_descendants` ancestor — and a plain `form_editor` passes `can:update,form` on any form they created (`FormService::create` mints them the editor grant). The column **is** in `Form::$fillable`, so the write is centralised in an explicit `forceFill` (`FormService::assignScope`) and a test pins that `PATCH /forms/{form}` cannot set it. **Presenter discipline:** two grouped aggregates, never `nodeReach()` per row (a prefix-LIKE per node against a hierarchy the resolver sizes at ~44k); `form_count` counts `withTrashed()` to agree with the impact modal opened from it. **Recipients are correct by construction, not by filtering** — the `users_visibility` RLS policy already admits only ACTIVE co-tenants plus self, so an invited member is a **404, not the `grant_recipient_inactive` toast** (`ResourceGrantServiceTest:113` says as much: RLS gets there first, the service guard is defence in depth) — the test asserts the real failure rather than encoding a fiction. Capacities render **disabled-with-reason, not hidden** (unreachable under today's 5-role matrix; forward-looking). **Pest 988 (+22)**; Vitest **414 unchanged**; Pint / controller-gate / migration-lint green; PHPStan **0 errors on all G10b2 files**; vue-tsc clean; **no `openapi.json` drift** (tenant-web routes are outside the surface). New `scopes-axe` spec **24/24 across all three viewports**, plus `/scopes` in `responsive-axe` (6/6). `E2eSeeder` gains a **5-level** hierarchy (deep enough that the 375px overflow case is actually reachable — a shallow fixture would make `responsive-axe` a false negative), forms at differing depths, a deactivated branch, a second **ACTIVE** member, and a descendant-inclusive reviewer grant. Narrowing: tree payload capped at 2,000 nodes with an honest notice (expand-on-demand is the follow-up); grants managed per node only.
- **G10b1 (as-built)** — the increment that makes the Reviewer role assignable. **Four decisions taken with the user up front:** one node per form (keep the `forms.scope_node_id` pointer, no pivot); fix the over-broad form list in this increment; **one** new `manage:scopes` ability mapped to both `scopes.manage` and `forms.collaborators.manage`; split G10b into backend (G10b1) + UI (G10b2). **`ScopeNodeService` gains `update`/`setActive`/`delete`/`move`/`deletionImpact`.** The keystone is **`move()`**: PROGRESS previously specified `FOR UPDATE` on node + target parent, which is **necessary but NOT sufficient** — a concurrent move of an *ancestor of the target* re-paths the target without locking it, so the first mover grafts its subtree onto a stale prefix, and `path` is exactly what authorization prefix-matches. Resolved with a **transaction-scoped advisory lock keyed on the tenant** (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(class, crc32(tenant))` — the two-int form, deliberately not the undocumented internal `hashtext`; the `_xact_` variant so a crashed request cannot strand it). It is the **only advisory lock in the codebase** (every other service serializes on a row) because it is the only operation whose correctness spans an unbounded row set. The `FOR UPDATE` re-reads stay and remain load-bearing (authoritative post-lock rows + blocking concurrent rename/deactivate). Then: cycle check as ONE self-inclusive prefix test (also covers move-onto-self, and is now the **only** thing preventing a longer cycle — `scope_nodes_no_self_parent` only ever covered the single-node case, so that migration's comment was corrected); depth cap measured across the **whole subtree** (a shallow node can carry a deep one); and a **single** `UPDATE` re-path over the constant-prefix `LIKE` (atomic, index-served by `(tenant_id, path)` under `COLLATE "C"`, one statement regardless of subtree size). **`ScopeNode::booted()` is KEPT VERBATIM, not relaxed** — the re-path is a query-builder statement so it fires no model events, meaning the guard still covers every ordinary save while the one audited writer sits behind the locks; an `unguarded`/re-entrancy flag was rejected as something a future caller can switch off. **A real PostgreSQL trap found in testing:** `substring(path from ?)` with an untyped bind resolves to the POSIX-**regex** overload `substring(text, text)` and returns NULL on no match — it would have nulled `path` on every touched row. Caught by NOT NULL; fixed to `substr(path, ?::int)` with both numeric binds cast explicitly. **New migration `2026_07_21_000001`** adds two CHECKs that make an incorrectly re-pathed row impossible *regardless of writer* (Eloquent, builder, raw SQL, a future migration): `scope_nodes_path_self_suffix_chk` (a path ends with its own id) and `scope_nodes_depth_matches_path_chk` (`depth` = path segment count, i.e. slashes − 2 — which also forces `path` and `depth` to be written in the same statement). **Node deletion now removes the subtree's grants in-transaction** — `resource_grants` is a morph with no FK, so nothing cascaded them and every delete silently manufactured orphaned authorization rows (this is cleanup at the creating site, *not* the deferred orphan sweeper), via a SUBQUERY not a `whereIn` (PG caps binds at 65535). **New `ResourceGrantService`** (grant/revoke; upserts so a demotion **replaces** rather than accumulates, per the unique key that deliberately excludes `capacity`; locks the re-resolved tenant-scoped target so concurrent grants cannot 23505-race; `->scopeable()->associate()` never mass-assignment) and **`ResourceGrantPolicy`** (registered explicitly — an unmapped model class fails **open**) with the escalation rules: base `forms.collaborators.manage`, **no self-grant**, and **anti-amplification** via the new `ResourceCapacity::anyPermission()` (editor→`forms.edit.any`, reviewer→`submissions.review.any`). Rule 3 is **unreachable under the shipped 5-role matrix** and is documented + tested against raw permission subsets rather than seeded roles, precisely so it is not filed as dead code the way `forms.collaborators.manage` itself was. **`ResourceGrantResolver::nodeReach()`** is the blast-radius counter, added *there* (not a parallel query) so the preview and the grant cannot disagree — it returns `{direct, descendant}`, reports **zero for a deactivated node or one under an inactive branch** (a preview advertising access the resolver will refuse is a lie an admin escalates on), and counts through the same `withTrashed()` scope the resolver uses. `escapeLike()` made public so the service shares one escaping rule. **`forget()` discipline documented + tested per writer** — the per-user variant clears ONLY `$sets`, leaving `$formPaths`/`$inactive` stale, so `move`/`setActive`/`delete` must all call the **argument-less** `forget()`. **The form-list leak was fixed on BOTH surfaces:** `FormPresenter::list()` *and* `GET /api/v1/forms` (`FormApiController::index`) had the identical "every non-archived tenant form to anyone passing the read gate" behaviour — fixing only the presenter would have moved the leak to the API rather than closed it; scoped via `grantedFormIdsQuery($user, Editor)` (Editor specifically: it is the authoring list). **API:** `manage:scopes` + 6 new paths (`/scopes`, `/scopes/{scopeNode}`, `/scopes/{scopeNode}/move` — deliberately separate from PATCH since it is a locked op with its own refusals — `/scopes/{scopeNode}/impact`, `/resource-grants`, `/resource-grants/{resourceGrant}`). **Every route pairs `ability:` with a `can:` policy gate**, which is what makes the single coarse ability safe (the map's any-of semantics would otherwise let a `scopes.manage`-only principal reach the grant routes) — recorded in `ApiAbilities::MANAGE_SCOPES` and api-spec §2.6 as a constraint on future routes. **Pest 966 (+64)**; Pint/controller-gate/migration-lint green; Larastan **0 errors** on all G10b files; vue-tsc green; `openapi.json` regenerated (23 paths), Redocly-valid, **no drift**. **The end-to-end walkthrough was run and all 18 checks passed** (4-level tree → grant on an interior node with descendants → move the subtree → deactivate an intermediate node → escalation refusals → deletion impact) — the exercise that, in G10a, found the bug the tests missed. Ships **backend-only**: no routes in `tenant.php`, no Vue, no Playwright (all G10b2).
- **G10a (as-built)** — Generalized `morphTo` resource-scoping, backend only. **G10 had NO design spec** — one roadmap bullet repeated in 5 places; the whole written contract is PRD §6 ("a generalized `morphTo`-based resource-scoping mechanism, the clean successor to the legacy PSGC/catchment-area hack"), PRD §7 (never a hardcoded geography table or a PHP switch-on-a-level-column; tenants model their own reference data), architecture §5 row 11 (`*_type` + `*_id`, resolved by the framework), and RBAC §8:215 nominating `form_collaborators`' two-layer composition as "the general pattern for any future resource that needs per-instance scoping beyond the tenant level". **Unified reading chosen with the user:** two tables. **`scope_nodes`** = a tenant-defined hierarchy with ZERO platform semantics (`name`/`code`/`node_type` are the tenant's own labels, never switched on) — adjacency list + a self-inclusive materialized `path` **`COLLATE "C"`** (load-bearing: descendant resolution is a constant-prefix `LIKE`, which PG compiles into indexable range quals only under C), depth CHECK 0–12, `is_active` instead of a `deleted_at` (a soft-delete the resolver must remember to filter is an authorization landmine), **composite** self-FK `(tenant_id, parent_id)` per ADR-0002 §D5 because **PG runs referential actions BYPASSING RLS**. **`resource_grants`** = the polymorphic grant (`scopeable` → `form` | `scope_node`, the repo's 2nd real `morphTo`; `Relation::morphMap` NOT `enforceMorphMap`), unique on `(tenant_id, scopeable_type, scopeable_id, user_id)` with **capacity deliberately NOT in the key** so a demotion replaces rather than adds, per-grant `includes_descendants` DEFAULT FALSE. `forms` gains `scope_node_id` (composite FK + PG15 **column-list** `SET NULL (scope_node_id)` — a plain composite SET NULL would null `tenant_id` and violate NOT NULL). **The security keystone: a morph has no FK, so under plain `strict` RLS an INSERT with my own `tenant_id` + another tenant's form id is ACCEPTED by Postgres** (verified: `strictSql()` emits only `WITH CHECK (tenant_id = ctx)`) — and unlike `attachments` that row is an authorization *decision input*. New **`TenantIsolation::resourceGrantGuard`** (6th variant, a new *instance* of the shipped `draftChildGuard` shape) adds a per-alias same-tenant `EXISTS` to INSERT/UPDATE `WITH CHECK`; SELECT/DELETE stay tenant-only so an orphaned grant stays revokable; **must be the SOLE write policy per command** (PG OR-combines same-command permissive policies — also calling `withTenantIsolation()` would silently restore bare tenant equality *and still pass CI*, so `TenantIsolationSqlTest` asserts exactly-one-policy-per-command). **`ResourceGrantResolver`** (`scoped()`, not `singleton()`) is the single interpreter of `resource_grants`, answering both `holds()` and the list twin `grantedFormIdsQuery()` from one grant set so policy and inbox cannot diverge; memo key is **(user, tenant)** — a user-only key bleeds across `enterTenant()` calls and makes authorization tests pass for the wrong reason. Resolution runs **downward from the grant**, never upward with a leading `%` (unindexable, and it sits in the inbox query); bounded by grant count, never descendant count (PG caps binds at 65535; a PSGC region is ~44k nodes). `withTrashed()` throughout is **behaviour preservation** — the old `FormCollaborator` subquery never joined `forms`, so a grant-holder always still saw soft-deleted forms' submissions. **Backfill**: runs on `pgsql_privileged` with `withinTransaction = false`, because migrations run as `meridian_app` with no tenant GUC → under FORCE RLS a naive `INSERT…SELECT` **reads nothing, writes nothing, throws nothing**, and the next migration would then drop the only copy of every access grant (a silent authorization wipe on a green log). Reuses `fc.id` as the grant PK (idempotent re-run; keeps UUIDv7 ordering), guards with `<` not `!=` (re-runs pass; `0<0` false so `migrate:fresh` passes), plus a post-flight cross-tenant re-check because the privileged connection *bypassed* the guard. **`form_collaborators` dropped**; its create-migration deliberately KEPT (`migrate:fresh` replays in order). **Adds `scopes.manage` — a 28th permission**, changing RBAC §3/§5's documented "fixed catalog of 27": reusing `tenant.settings.manage` would be actively harmful since `ApiAbilities` maps `manage:settings` onto it, retroactively widening every already-minted token. **One deliberate behaviour change:** `SubmissionPolicy::create` (manual encoding) now requires **Editor** capacity, not either — at subtree scale a reviewer grant on an interior node would otherwise confer write access to every form beneath it. Everything else is behaviour-identical **by construction**: after backfill every grant targets a form, every `scope_node_id` is NULL, and G10a ships no writer for either, so the node branch is a constant-false conjunct — **the pre-existing suite is the regression gate, and it passed unchanged**. **Found by the END-TO-END walkthrough, not by the tests** (which is why the plan required driving a real hierarchy through the resolver): deactivating an **intermediate** node did not cut off its descendants, because `is_active` was only checked on the granted node and the form's own node — making it a control that *looks* like it revokes a branch but doesn't, i.e. exactly the landmine the no-`deleted_at` decision exists to prevent. Fixed by loading the tenant's inactive paths once and reusing the same prefix machinery, applied to **both** query shapes (+2 tests). **Pest 902 (+84)**; Larastan 78 errors = exact `main` baseline with **zero in G10a files**; Pint/migration-lint/controller-gate/vue-tsc green; **no `openapi.json` drift** (zero routes). Ships **backend-only** — no routes, controllers, or UI. **All 6 CI jobs green on the FIRST push** (G9a and G9b each needed 3 follow-up fixes).

- **G8c (as-built)** — the 409 conflict-resolution UX, the last G8 slice. **Frontend (reuse the fill screen):** the parked `outbox` `status:'conflict'` rows now get a **Review** CTA on `SyncStatus.vue` (`ConflictReviewKey`). `App.vue` `beginConflictReview()` re-mints + re-fetches the current schema, then remounts `RuntimeSession` seeded with the saved answers (existing `restoreAnswers` drops stale keys + mints a fresh uuid) + a resolve notice — the same online-drift machinery, sourced from a parked row instead of a live `reschema`. On a successful resubmit the old conflict row is discarded (`discardConflict`); a "discard this response" hatch also exists. `RuntimeSession` gains a `resolving` mode that **suppresses autosave** (new `enabled` guard on `useAutosave` — the durable copy is the outbox row, so a transient review can't clobber a live `[form_version_id+slug]` draft) and hides `SyncStatus`. New outbox helpers: `listConflicts(slug)`, `nextConflict()`/`discardConflict()` on `useSyncOutbox` (interactive resolution is **current-form-slug-scoped** — App's single share-token client is bound to `bootstrap.slug`; the global conflict count may exceed what "Review" acts on). `conflict_code` on `OutboxRow` records which 409 parked it (non-indexed → **no Dexie version bump**). **Backend (content-checksum 409):** new nullable `submission_answers.answers_content_checksum` + `AnswersContentChecksum` (canonical SHA-256, key-sorted, list-order-preserving). `SubmissionPipeline` hashes the **normalized** answers; on a duplicate `client_submission_uuid` it compares stored-vs-incoming — match (or a legacy `null`) → the existing **200** idempotent no-op, differ → new `SubmissionConflictException` → **`409 submission_conflict`** (a distinct code from the version-drift `submission_version_superseded`; `bootstrap/app.php` render closure). Batch `sync/submissions` maps it per-item. Both 409 causes converge on the same client resolution flow (only the notice copy differs). **Documented narrowing:** no server-side merge — the 409 body doesn't return the server's answers, so resolution resubmits the user's **local** copy as a fresh response; the server's copy is left for inbox reconciliation. In the guest replay path a content conflict is a defensive/multi-channel edge (queued rows are immutable) — its everyday effect is making idempotency content-aware.
- **G8b (as-built)** — the offline engine proper. One **Dexie** DB (`resources/public-runtime/lib/db.ts`, 4 stores: `cached_manifests`/`draft_answers`/`outbox`/`media_queue`; `plainClone()` strips Vue reactive proxies before every IndexedDB write). One framework-free **`replayOutbox(db,fetch)`** (`lib/replay.ts`) imported by **both** the app driver and the SW, reusing the stateful `ApiClient`: `201/200`→synced, `422`→needs_attention, `409`→conflict (G8c), network-fail→retry w/ 5-attempt escalation; media uploads **first**, rewrites `local:` placeholder refs → real attachment ids, then POSTs. **Service worker switched `generateSW`→`injectManifest`** (`sw.ts`): re-expresses the 3 G8a runtime caches in Workbox code + owns a Background-Sync `sync` handler for no-tab replay; Rollup bundles Workbox+Dexie into one **classic** `sw.js` (no CSP change), type-checked under a WebWorker-lib **`tsconfig.sw.json`** (wired into `npm run type-check`). App driver **`useSyncOutbox.ts`** fires replay on `online`/`visibilitychange`/manual **"Sync now"** (the iOS-reliable path) + `navigator.storage.estimate()` quota warning; **`SyncStatus.vue`** surfaces queued/needs-attention/conflict counts. **`RuntimeSession.submit()`** now enqueues to the outbox FIRST (durable intent), then replays inline (online) or queues (offline → "saved on this device" confirmation). **UUIDv7** `client_submission_uuid` (`lib/uuid.ts`; ULID rejected — fails the `uuid` column+rule); **localStorage autosave migrated to Dexie `draft_answers`** (async restore + one-time migration). Offline media: `MediaInput.vue` stashes a blob when offline → `local:` placeholder (admin encode path untouched, guarded by an injected `OfflineMediaKey`). Backend: **`device_id`/`app_version` wired** through `SubmissionPayload`→`persist()` + guest request/controller (both dormant columns now populated). New authenticated **Group-B sync API**: `GET /api/v1/sync/manifest` (pinned snapshot+checksum) + `POST /api/v1/sync/submissions` (idempotent batch, per-item `created`/`duplicate`/`invalid`/`conflict`/`error`); the guest PWA replays via the **guest** endpoints (hybrid channel). **Documented narrowing:** media replay is whole-file + per-file retry, not the design-doc resumable-multipart. **G8c defers:** the 409 conflict-resolution UX (reuses the emitted `submission_version_superseded` / `form_updated`; optional answers-content-checksum 409).
- **G8a (as-built)** — the guest public-runtime SPA is now an installable PWA that renders a *previously-loaded* form offline. `vite-plugin-pwa` (Workbox `generateSW`, one `vite.config.ts`) emits a self-contained `sw.js` with precache **disabled** (`globPatterns:[]`) + 3 `runtimeCaching` routes (`/build/*` SWR, `/api/v1/public/f/*` schema NetworkFirst, `/f/*` nav NetworkFirst) so the Inertia admin bundle is never cached. New `ServiceWorkerController` re-serves the worker from **`GET /sw.js`** (root scope) so `main.ts` registers it `{scope:'/f/'}` — admin app untouched. New `PwaManifestController` serves a **per-form** manifest at `GET /f/{slug}/manifest.webmanifest` (id/start_url/scope=`/f/{slug}`), same `public_slug` + 3×404 gates as the mint route; icons in `public/icons/`. CSP (`PublicRuntimeSecurityHeaders`) gains `worker-src`/`manifest-src 'self'` (script/connect stay unset). Offline UX = `useOnline` + `OfflineIndicator` pill + a submit pre-flight guard (no outbox yet — the localStorage autosave holds the draft). **G8b defers:** Dexie outbox, Background-Sync replay, media blob queue, `device_id`/`app_version` wiring; **G8c defers:** the 409 conflict UX (reuses the emitted `submission_version_superseded`).
- **G7b (as-built)** — new in **`app/Services/Xlsform/`**: `XlsformWorkbookReader` (openspout read), `XlsformImportParser` (pure, DB-free, validates **fully upfront**), `CascadeResolver` (reconstructs **both** our own marker+`level`/`parent` cascades AND foreign multi-question `choice_filter` chains → one `cascading_select`; non-linear degrades + warns), `XlsformImporter` (destructive draft-replace à la `RestoreService`: lock → delete validations/fields/sections → recreate with **explicit keys** + key→id FK remap + `created_by`), `XlsformImportException` (`xlsform_missing_survey_sheet` / `xlsform_unsupported_field_type` + `{row,type}`, upfront) + `bootstrap/app.php` render closure. Reuses G7a's `toFieldType`/`isImportable` + `wireToGeoJson`. Import runs **no** publish gates (author reviews → publishes). Documented narrowings + warnings (interop-spec §8): `yes_no`/`likert_scale`→`single_select`, `duration`→`decimal`, `matrix`/`likert_matrix` not importable, dynamic `repeat_count`→unbounded, illegal `name`→sanitized key; `constraint` always an expression row. Both surfaces: tenant-web builder Import modal + warnings banner (new `upload` icon) + API-v1 `POST /forms/{form}/draft/xlsform-import`. **Keystone `XlsformRoundTripTest`** = export→import→publish round-trips by key.
- **G7a (as-built)** — `XlsformTypeMap` (bidirectional type table), `GeoWireConverter` (single lat/lon-flip site, ADR-0006 §3.1), `XlsformWorkbookWriter`, `XlsformExporter` (reads the id-free `SchemaSnapshotSerializer` snapshot; **no DB in the stream closure**). 0. **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-07 — the I5 verification debt is retired, and it was NOT merely procedural.** Kept as a short record because of what it caught. I5 merged on 2026-08-06 with 2 of 6 CI jobs confirmed during a GitHub Actions **major outage**; Static, Contract and Pest were attempted **four times** and cancelled every time having executed **ZERO STEPS** — they never acquired a runner. Actions returned to `operational` on 08-07, and the very first run that actually executed returned **two real failures**:

   - **Two `TenantMaintenanceTest` cases were missing `$this->withoutVite()`** — the only two in that file that render the real guest shell (`public-runtime.blade.php`, which carries `@vite`) and expect a 200. The three paused cases render the maintenance blade, which is why they were green. The Pest job never runs `npm run build`, so `@vite` throws and the 200 arrives as a **500**. They passed locally only because `public/build` happened to exist. **These were I5 defects that CI had never once been allowed to run.**
   - **Three `league/commonmark` advisories** (incl. CVE-2026-71488, quadratic-time DoS, high) published 08-06 20:30–20:39 UTC — after that session's clean local `composer audit` and before CI ran it. Transitive; bumped 2.8.2 → 2.9.0, `composer.json` untouched.

   **THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS ENTRY SURVIVES ITS OWN CLOSURE: an unverified gate is not a green gate.** Local runs said 2757/2757 and clean audits on both trees; CI still found two genuine problems, one of them a live high-severity advisory that could not have existed at local-run time. **Do not merge to `main` on the strength of local gates alone**, however complete they look. **How to tell an outage from a real failure** — an outage-killed job reports `conclusion: cancelled` with **`steps: []`**; a real failure has steps with a failed one among them. Both at once: `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/runs/<id>/jobs --jq '.jobs[]|"\(.name): \(.conclusion) steps=\(.steps|length)"'`.

   **Confirmed green 6/6 twice on 2026-08-07** — on PR #105 (run `31135664351`) and on the post-merge integration state `4364fbe` (run `31136437602`), which contains I5's code and is therefore its re-verification. `ci.yml`'s `workflow_dispatch` + nightly `schedule` remain **inert until the file reaches `main`** (GitHub reads those triggers from the default branch only), so `gh run rerun` stays the control until the final integration PR.
1. **Build order:** ~~I5~~ → ~~I6~~ → ~~I7a~~ → ~~I7b~~ → ~~I8a~~ → ~~I8b~~ → ~~I8c~~ → ~~I9a~~ → `I9b → I9c → I10 → I11 → I12`, then **H16b** (Sheets UI), then **H16c** (Airtable), then the **OCR chain** (H1d → H18a/b → H19a/b), then **Track B** (deployment), then **payments**. Nothing in that list waits on the user except where noted below. **I9 is THREE PRs** (split with the user 2026-08-08): I9a ScreenedOut + the capacity bug, I9b encode save-as-draft, I9c post-submission editing.

2. **OCR goes last, and it costs NOTHING to defer.** Verified rather than assumed: H18a/b and H19a/b are PRD Features #1 and #2, and **nothing in I5–I12 depends on them** — they are a leaf. The dependency runs the other way: **I12 builds the blank-form print renderer, which is how the user produces the filled scans**, so OCR could not precede I12 even with samples in hand. H19a's *second* blocker (H16a's shared drift engine) cleared when #99 merged. The honest caveat, stated so nobody is surprised: those are the PRD's first two features, so at testing time everything else exists and OCR does not.

3. **Google credentials are SUPPLIED and verified loaded** (`GOOGLE_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID` 72 chars ending `googleusercontent.com`, secret 35 chars, scope exactly `drive.file`, adapter resolvable). The user also set `CONNECTOR_TENANT_URL_SCHEME=http` locally. **H16b and H16c are UN-PARKED** — the Sheets UI needs no live credentials to build (with `drive.file` there is no server-side spreadsheet enumeration, hence no `channel_lister`), and Airtable builds against a fake exactly as Slack did in H15a. `AIRTABLE_CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` do not exist yet — add them WITH H16c and tell the user then.

4. **⚠️ H16b MUST FIX THE REDIRECT-URI PORT, and the obvious fix is a trap.** The app emits `http://localhost/oauth/google_sheets/callback`; the user registered `http://localhost:8080/oauth/google_sheets/callback` (correct — Google accepts `http://localhost`, and `.env.example` has always documented the port-carrying local form). `ConnectorRedirector::centralHost()` returns `tenancy.central_domain` with NO port, deliberately — its own docblock argues it, because the redirect URI must match the provider console byte-for-byte and production carries no port. **DO NOT "fix" this by putting a port in `CENTRAL_DOMAIN`:** that env var feeds BOTH `tenancy.central_domain` and the `central_domains` array (`config/tenancy.php:30` and `:38`), and `Route::domain()` matches `Request::getHost()`, which STRIPS the port — so `localhost:8080` there silently deletes the super-admin console route, the connector callback route ITSELF, the platform-host guard and subdomain tenant identification. The fix is in `ConnectorRedirector` (derive the port from `app.url`) plus pinning `APP_URL` in `phpunit.xml`, which currently forces `CENTRAL_DOMAIN=meridian.test` while `APP_URL` inherits `localhost:8080` — the test env contradicts itself. Attempted during the #99 merge and REVERTED: it breaks ~10 asserted redirects in `ConnectorOAuthFlowTest` and does not belong in a merge commit (the I2 → I8 precedent).

5. **⚠️ Google is on Testing publishing status, deliberately** — and **refresh tokens issued in Testing expire after 7 DAYS**. `access_type=offline` plus the hourly `RefreshConnectorTokensJob` cannot save a grant Google has expired, so a Sheets connection will die weekly until the app is published at Track B. **H16b must say so in the UI** rather than render a bare "reconnect needed", or it reads as a token-handling bug in our code. Publishing later should be uneventful: `drive.file` is Google's *Non-sensitive* tier and needs no verification review — which is exactly why H16a chose it over `spreadsheets`.

6. **Payments stay cut to Phase 4** — the user reconfirmed: revisit only after the form builder and data collection have been tested. **Track B is last**, after the app is otherwise deployment-ready.

7. **PRs #97 and #99 are CLOSED/MERGED — there are no open PRs left.** #97 (H23a4) was closed as redundant: its commits were folded into `phase1-completion` during I0, verified with `git merge-base --is-ancestor`. #99 (H16a) was retargeted from `main` to `phase1-completion` and merged, resolving four conflicts by hand — including a threat-model row where BOTH sides had recorded something true (I2's widened blast radius AND H16a's `valueInputOption=RAW` addendum) and the facts were merged rather than one side picked.

**NEW LOCAL-GATE LESSON (2026-08-06):** **a green local Vitest is NOT proof that every test ran.** A worker occasionally fails to START in this Docker setup; when it does, that file's tests never run and never appear in the total, while the run still reports the remaining files as passed. Observed as 1284 → 1270 → 1292 across three runs of the same tree. **Check the FILE COUNT (64), not just pass/fail**, and treat CI as the authority.

**I7b (do not re-open):** **THE GENERIC SUPER-ADMIN BYPASS IS WRONG ON `audits`, AND IT IS ALWAYS ONE LINE AWAY.** `TenantIsolation::applySuperAdminBypass('audits', ['SELECT'])` would have shipped a working platform audit viewer immediately — and handed the operator **every tenant's complete history** (every form title, every reviewer's `returned_reason`, every membership change in the deployment) in order to display a handful of platform-settings rows. **The helper's gate is a DEFAULT, NOT A CONTRACT**: it is correct only where the base shape is `nullable_global` (the tenant slice is already public there) and wrong on anything strict or append-only. `audits` therefore took a new NAMED generator, **`TenantIsolation::platformRowsBypass()`**, emitting `audits_platform_select` with `… AND tenant_id IS NULL`. **The plan said HAND-WRITE that policy; ADR-0002 §D2 says policies are generated by a reusable helper "not hand-written per table", and the user chose the generator** — the narrowing was the real requirement, hand-writing was only the means. ADR-0002 §D3 now carries the amendment. **THE POLICY NAME IS `audits_platform_select`, DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE THE `audits_superadmin_%` PREFIX** the older bypass migration sweeps in its `down()`: a shared prefix lets an unrelated rollback drop this policy while its `GRANT SELECT` survives, and the page then renders **zero rows with no error** under copy saying nobody has ever performed a platform action — a plausible lie on a compliance surface. It also keeps a `pg_policies` grep for `%_superadmin_%` an honest answer to "where does the operator hold an unrestricted read". **MUTATION-VERIFIED, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT PROVED NOTHING WHILE LOOKING LIKE IT DID.** Mutating the LIVE database is useless — `RefreshDatabase` runs `migrate:fresh` and rebuilds the correct policy underneath you, so the suite stayed green on a deliberately broken database. **The migration FILE is what must be mutated.** Done properly: swapping the generator for `applySuperAdminBypass()` reddens three `PlatformAuditRlsTest` cases including "never lets the elevated role read a TENANT-scoped audit row"; removing `withoutGlobalScope(Audit::$tenantScope)` reddens 9 of 11 `PlatformAuditReadTest` cases. **`whereNull('tenant_id')` IS NOT REDUNDANT WITH THE POLICY** and is three things at once: the ORM half of the isolation, defence in depth, and the query plan — `audits_tenant_select` has no `TO` clause so it applies to the elevated role too, Postgres OR-composes same-command permissive policies, and only a user-level leakproof `IS NULL` lets the planner keep the ordered scan on `audits_tenant_recent_idx`. "The policy already does that" is exactly how it gets deleted. **TWO LIVE 500s WERE FOUND AND FIXED, both latent since H5a purely because no UI ever called those routes:** `GET /admin/tenants/not-a-uuid` reached Postgres as `where id = 'not-a-uuid'` on a uuid column → **SQLSTATE 22P02, a 500 not a 404** (fixed with `->whereUuid('tenant')`), and `plan_id=garbage` did the same through `exists:plans,id`. **`'uuid'` ALONE DOES NOT FIX THE SECOND ONE** — Laravel short-circuits an attribute only after an *implicit* rule fails, so the rule set must be `['required','bail','uuid','exists:plans,id']`. Both mutation-verified against the real 22P02. **`EntitlementService::forget()` IS CALLED ON BOTH SIDES OF `tenantSnapshot()`, AND THIS TRACKER'S OWN STATED REASON FOR IT WAS WRONG.** It said the memo would carry one tenant's plan "into the rest of the render"; that cannot happen — inertia-laravel calls `Inertia::share()` BEFORE the controller, and `currentPlan()` early-returns on a null tenant id before it ever consults the memo. The real reason is the TEST process: the service is bound `scoped()` and **only the queue worker calls `forgetScopedInstances()`**, so one instance survives every `$this->get()`/`$this->post()` in a Pest run, and a render→mutate→render test would otherwise assert a stale plan **in the passing direction**. **EVERY FAILURE MODE ON THE DETAIL PAGE IS SILENT.** Move any read out of the adopt-context transaction and `storage_bytes` reads 0, `active_seats` reads 0, the owner reads null, the plan reads null — nothing throws. The owner assertion and the "usage reads the right tenant" assertion exist for exactly that. **`MdsModal`'s slot is `actions`; `MdsCard`'s is `footer`** — using `#footer` on a modal renders the confirm buttons NOWHERE, silently, and a Vitest case is what caught it. **THE ADMIN NAV OVERFLOWS BELOW ~900px AND ALWAYS DID.** `.admin__bar` is a fixed-height 56px flex row and the breakpoint that reflows it was 480px; five nav links measure ~860px against a ~720px content box at 768px, and the four pre-I7b links were already ~767px. Nothing in CI can see it — `/admin/*` is excluded from the Playwright sweep because `superadmin.mfa` needs a TOTP. Breakpoint raised to 900px, and the coverage gap is now a named residual in the threat model. **`clearPlatformRows()` / `committedSuperAdmin()` MOVED FROM `PlatformSettingsWriteTest` INTO `tests/Pest.php`** — Pest loads every file into one process, so a second declaration is a fatal redeclare. **THE FIXTURE PURGE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: audits → tenants → users.** `audits.tenant_id` is `nullOnDelete`, so deleting a fixture tenant first does not delete its audit rows — it PROMOTES them into permanent platform rows the viewer under test then displays, this run and every run after. The same mechanism is a real product consequence: hard-deleting a tenant moves its whole history onto `/admin/audit-log` (now recorded in data-dictionary §13 and the threat model). **TENANT-DETAIL TESTS USE ORDINARY FACTORIES, NOT I7a's COMMITTED FIXTURES** — nothing on that page elevates, so the committed apparatus would be pure cost, and registering `purgeCommittedFeedbackFixtures()` there would sweep `slug LIKE 'console-%'` across unrelated suites. **⚠️ LOCAL PHPSTAN ON THIS MACHINE IS BLIND TO ELOQUENT MODEL PROPERTIES AND REPORTS ~64 PHANTOM ERRORS; CI REPORTS `[OK] No errors` ON THE SAME TREE** — same larastan v3.10.0, same PHP 8.4, vendor synced to `composer.lock`. Every one is a `property.notFound` on a model or an `unresolvableType` downstream of one; confirmed with a two-method probe where the method touching a model property errored and the identical method without one did not. **Do not chase them and do not "fix" code to satisfy them — but do NOT trust a green local run either, and this is the half that cost real time.** The blindness cuts BOTH ways: an unresolvable model type degrades to `mixed`, and `mixed` SILENCES downstream errors. On the first push CI rejected three genuine errors this container had reported as clean — a nullsafe call on a non-nullable `Carbon`, a redundant `is_string()`, and an `array` passed where `list<string>|null` was required. **Local PHPStan on this machine is not a weak signal, it is not a signal at all.** Budget one CI round-trip for static analysis on any increment that touches model properties or array shapes derived from them. Measure the DELTA against the parent branch instead — `phpstan analyse --error-format=json` on each branch, diff by `basename|message`. I7b's measured delta was 0 — and CI still found 3, because the errors that mattered were the ones local could not compute at all. The tell is that the phantom errors concentrate on model property access; the hidden ones do too.

**I7a (do not re-open):** **THE SUPER-ADMIN RLS CARVE-OUT IS ONLY THE DATABASE HALF, AND THE OTHER HALF FAILS SILENTLY.** `BelongsToTenant` adds its OWN global scope — `where tenant_id = <context ?? NO_TENANT_SENTINEL>` — to every Eloquent query, independently of RLS. The console runs on the central host with **no** tenant context, so `listFeedback()`/`findFeedback()` asked the database for rows belonging to a tenant that cannot exist: an empty list, no error, under an empty state reading "No feedback yet". Both now call `withoutGlobalScope(FeedbackReport::$tenantScope)`. **`listAllUsers()` escaped this only because `users` has no tenant column**, so every FUTURE console surface over a tenant-scoped table inherits the requirement. The test half matters just as much: `FeedbackRlsTest` drops the ORM scope too, because leaving it on would make those assertions pass whether the Postgres policies existed or not — a bypass written without its `TO meridian_superadmin` clause would have sailed straight through a green suite. **ROUTE-MODEL BINDING CANNOT BE USED ON A CONSOLE ROUTE OVER AN RLS TABLE** for the same root cause: binding resolves on the app connection, so every valid id 404s. `/admin/feedback/{feedback}` takes a raw uuid and resolves through the service; `/admin/tenants/{tenant}` gets away with binding only because `tenants` is RLS-exempt. **THE BYPASS IS `['SELECT']` AND MUST STAY THAT WAY.** Transitions deliberately do NOT elevate — the console already knows the report's tenant, so `transitionFeedback()` uses the H4 adopt-context pattern, which is the ONLY reason the audit row lands in the **reporting workspace's own** ledger where RBAC §9 says it must be visible. An UPDATE bypass would write `tenant_id = NULL` and hide the operator's handling from exactly the people entitled to see it. **`attachments` HAS NO BYPASS AT ALL, DELIBERATELY** — the console reads a screenshot by adopting the report's tenant context; a bypass there would hand the operator every respondent file and every OCR scan in the deployment in order to display one image. `FeedbackRlsTest` asserts both absences by querying `pg_policies`, so a later "for symmetry" addition fails there first. **THE AUDIT ALIAS IS THE PLURAL `feedback_reports`** — matching `AuditRedactor::PII` and `AuditableTypes::LABELS`, unlike the singular `submission`/`attachment`. The singular writes a perfectly valid-looking row while silently switching redaction OFF for `remarks`/`browser_info`. **CREATION IS NOT AUDITED AND THAT IS THE SPEC'S ANSWER**, not a judgement call: audit spec §1 registers exactly one event for this table, `updated`. **`Tenant::on('pgsql_privileged')` DOES NOT WORK AND FAILS SILENTLY** — stancl's `CentralConnection` trait makes `getConnectionName()` ignore every override, so the row lands on the default connection inside `RefreshDatabase`'s transaction and the failure surfaces later, elsewhere, as an FK violation. Committed tenant fixtures must use the query builder. **ONE CONSOLE TRANSITION PER TEST** — a second PATCH re-reads through the elevated connection and sees the ORIGINAL committed status, so it is validated against the wrong `from` and usually refused *silently*, because a refusal is also a redirect-back. Assert `assertSessionHasNoErrors()`, never a bare `assertRedirect()`. **THE SCREENSHOT DECISION IS ADR-0015 AND THE PRD NOTE IS CLOSED** — native `getDisplayMedia`, zero dependencies, with the file input rendered ALWAYS rather than as a failure fallback; do not re-open the html2canvas option (it renders the Leaflet picker and the signature canvas blank). **`--mds-color-border-subtle` DOES NOT EXIST** — `token-references.test.ts`, the suite this file once called a known flake, caught it. Use `--mds-color-border-default`. **A COMMITTED CROSS-CONNECTION FIXTURE MUST BE PURGED, AND `SuperAdminBypassTest`'s "do not clean up" NOTE DOES NOT TRANSFER.** It commits `users`; these suites commit **tenants**, and nine unrelated tests went red in CI on a green local tree because of it. `purgeCommittedFeedbackFixtures()` (tests/Pest.php) is registered via `$this->beforeApplicationDestroyed(...)` from `beforeEach` — RefreshDatabase registers its rollback the same way during `setUp`, i.e. earlier, and Laravel runs them in order, so the DELETE happens after the transaction is gone and cannot deadlock. Key the purge on MARKERS, never ids, so a test that dies mid-way is cleaned by the next one. **THE TENANT `/feedback` PAGE IS READ-ONLY ON PURPOSE**: Feature #11 gives the lifecycle to the platform team, and a tenant marking its own report Resolved would be resolving it in the operator's queue.

**I6 (do not re-open):** **THE `/` ROUTE IS ONE ROUTE ON PURPOSE, AND THE OBVIOUS "FIX" BREAKS A PINNED TEST.** Do not add `Route::domain(config('tenancy.central_domain'))` to `routes/web.php` and move the tenant root into `routes/tenant.php`. The load-order half of the old note was right — `web.php` registers ~70 slots ahead of anything in `tenant.php` (mapped from `TenancyServiceProvider` inside `app->booted()`), so a `Route::get('/')` there is dead code — but the constraint that would free it up also stops `/` matching **custom** hosts, at which point the tenant route answers them and `InitializeTenancyBySubdomain` throws `NotASubdomainException` → **302 to `app.url`**, where `CustomDomainRoutingTest:158-167` pins a **404**. A host the platform does not own must not learn that a path exists, and the redirect would additionally disclose the canonical hostname. It would also diverge `localhost` from `127.0.0.1` locally, since both sit in `central_domains` while only one is `central_domain`. `RequirePlatformHost` already draws exactly the right line, so `PlatformLandingController` branches on `PlatformHost::subdomainLabel()`/`tenantFor()` **inside** the existing route; `PlatformLandingTest`'s last case asserts "404, **and specifically not a redirect**" so the change fails loudly rather than quietly. **`$request->user()` IS SAFE THERE WITH NO TENANT GUC** and this looks wrong next to `OpenTenantRegistrationTest`, which records that a per-tenant *settings* question in that state fails closed and silently: identity is the exception, because `config/auth.php`'s `users` provider is **`rls_aware`** and resolves on `pgsql_auth` precisely so login/registration/reset work against the fail-closed join-shape policy. **Do not add `EstablishTenantDatabaseContext` to that route.** The closure became a controller because a route **closure makes `route:cache` throw**. **`Welcome.vue` STAYS SELF-LAID-OUT** — `app.ts` excludes it by name, DSR §3.0's two shells do not fit a guest hero (`AuthLayout` is ~400px), and it is logged as **exceptions-log #8**; it is the first and only consumer of `--mds-type-display` / `--mds-space-16` / `--mds-space-24`, which the DSR has reserved for this class of surface since G11. Its `registrationOpen` prop must stay: without it the front door offers a button `GateRegistration` 404s. **`DatabaseSeeder`'s `WithoutModelEvents` MUST NOT COME BACK** — it wraps the whole run and every `->call()`ed child in `Model::withoutEvents()`, killing `HasUuidv7::creating` (the uuid PK; `users.id` has no DB default) and `BelongsToTenant::creating` (`tenant_id`, without which strict RLS refuses the row). There are **zero Observer classes in this repository**, so it protected nothing and broke `migrate:fresh --seed` for months, unseen because **nothing had ever executed that file**; `DatabaseSeederSmokeTest` is now the thing that would catch it. **`DemoSeeder` MUST NEVER WRITE INTO `acme`** — `E2eSeederIdempotencyTest` pins that fixture's counts exactly, and coupling a convenience fixture to a merge-blocking gate is how an unrelated PR starts failing. **THE AUDIT TAIL'S BACK-DATING IS AN INSERT, NOT AN UPDATE, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE PERMISSION** — `audits` is `append_only` (strict SELECT + strict INSERT, **no UPDATE/DELETE policy at all**), so `forceFill(...)->saveQuietly()` is structurally impossible there while supplying `created_at` on the INSERT is fine. **Do not add an `occurredAt` parameter to `AuditLogger`**; `E2eSeeder.php:1096-1099` forbids it and this did not need it. `redacted_fields` on those rows is **produced by `AuditRedactor`**, never authored — the same discipline `AnswerIndexProjector` enforces for the index, and for the same reason: a hand-written redaction list is a fiction the viewer then renders as fact. **THE REVIEWER'S GRANT SET DELIBERATELY EXCLUDES `Patient Intake`** — it carries ~420 of the ~520 rows, so granting it made the reviewer see 446 where an Owner sees 497, which is correct and completely invisible; excluding it makes it 104 vs 497. `reviewer` and `form_editor` lack `dashboard.org.view`, so **without seeded grants their inbox is empty and the guide fails on its first review step**. **`resolveOrCreateUser()`'s PER-RUN MEMO IS LOAD-BEARING** — `consultant@demo.test` is resolved twice in one `run()`, and under `RefreshDatabase` the second `pgsql_auth` lookup cannot see the first, uncommitted, insert; without the memo it 23505s **in tests only**, for a seeder that works perfectly from the CLI. **`$historyDays` IS ONLY SOUND BECAUSE EVERY DERIVED VALUE IS A FUNCTION OF THE ROW INDEX, NEVER THE ROW COUNT** — that is what makes a short spec a genuine prefix of a long one, and `it('derives every submission row from its index')` pins it directly; if a formula ever divides by the count, the cheap tests stop proving anything about what ships. **Two corrections to this file's own earlier claims, both verified:** `phpunit.xml` pins Pest to **`meridian_testing`**, so the suite CANNOT wipe the dev database — only Playwright can, because it serves from `.env`; and the settings surface carries eleven cards (Profile · Appearance · Notifications · Drafts · Access · Maintenance · Modules · Custom domains · Branding · Security · About), with **no bare `GET /admin` route** at all (the console's entry point is `/admin/tenants`). **`docs/TESTING-GUIDE.md` IS NOW A MERGE-BLOCKING DOC** in the same sense the data dictionary is: an increment that changes a surface the guide walks must update the guide in the same PR, and §17's "what is not built" table must lose the row it just built.

**I4 (do not re-open):** **THE FEED IS A JSON SIDECAR, AND THAT SUPERSEDES THIS FILE'S OWN EARLIER INSTRUCTION.** The I-map and the previous NEXT PROMPT both said "unread count as a **LAZY** Inertia prop"; that is now wrong, and the reason is measured rather than stylistic. An `Inertia::optional()` prop is refreshed by a partial reload, and **a partial reload RE-DISPATCHES THE CURRENT PAGE'S CONTROLLER** — Inertia filters what it SERIALIZES, not what it COMPUTES. `AuditLogPresenter::index()` and `SubmissionInboxPresenter::list()` both paginate eagerly, so a 60-second tick with `/audit-log` open would buy a full ledger paginate plus a `count(*)` and discard it, forever, in every open tab. `GET /notifications` → `{unread_count, items[15]}` instead, and **nothing about notifications enters `HandleInertiaRequests::share()`**. Do not "fix" this back into a prop. **Popover-only** — no `/notifications` page and no nav item; the bell IS the centre, and PRD #13 asks for "recent events". **Both writes answer 204 through `builderClient`**, a NAMED EXCEPTION to the "every web-route mutation is an Inertia visit" rule that `scopesClient`/`integrationsClient` state — that rule exists because `bootstrap/app.php` keys its domain-exception branch on the `api/v1/*` PATH, and neither of these routes raises one. The reason it MUST be a fetch: Inertia's request stream is `maxConcurrent: 1, interruptible: true`, so a `router.post()` fired in the same tick as the row's `<Link>` navigation is **silently aborted** — click-to-read is unimplementable as an Inertia visit without a wasted second round trip. **In-app row copy is built SERVER-side** in `NotificationCopy::inApp()`, and I3's docblock claiming the opposite is amended: `data` is a six-arm discriminated union only PHP authors, where an exhaustive `match` makes an eighth case a FATAL and a TS `default:` arm makes it a blank row nobody notices. TS owns the icon and the colour band and nothing else. **A ROW'S `url` IS REACHABILITY-RESOLVED, NOT MERELY SLASH-JOINED, AND THAT IS NOT DEFENSIVENESS.** `SubmissionPolicy::view()` is `submissions.view && (org-wide || collaborates on the form)` with **no respondent clause**, yet `submission_approved`/`submission_returned` are addressed to `respondent_user_id` — so a form editor whose grant is later revoked keeps a row pointing at Laravel's **bare 403 page, outside the Inertia shell, with no navigation back**. `/webhooks/{id}` additionally needs `feature:webhooks`, and all three target tables soft-delete. The presenter batch-loads the targets, runs the real `Gate`, and ships `url: null` otherwise, so the row renders as plain text. **Widening the policy is I9's call** — `docs/feature-backlog.md` §4 carries the row; do not do it as a drive-by. **No permission key and no `feature:` gate, both asserted by `NotificationRouteGuardsTest`** so neither can be tidied into symmetry with the four gated neighbours: a notification is addressed to ONE person by `user_id`, so authorization is `scopeForUser()`, and PRD #13 says notifications ship on **every** tier. **`Notification::scopeForUser()` IS THE AUTHORIZATION** on the list, on the unread count and on the mark-all UPDATE — strict RLS scopes to the TENANT only, so an unscoped mark-all silently clears every member's inbox, unaudited; `NotificationReadTest` pins it from both sides. The per-row write is gated on the BOUND INSTANCE by `NotificationPolicy::markRead`, the `SavedReportViewPolicy::owns()` case verbatim. **THE POLL STOPS AFTER 30 IDLE TICKS, AND THAT IS A SECURITY DECISION I8 DEPENDS ON** — every poll touches the session, so an eternal one keeps an abandoned tab authenticated forever and deletes `config/session.php`'s 120-minute idle expiry. It also tears the timer down entirely while the tab is hidden, and a failed poll NEVER blanks the bell (null means "ask again later", not "you have nothing"). **The E2E fixture keeps one row on the REVIEWER on purpose** — Playwright only logs in as the Owner, so a regression that dropped `scopeForUser()` would be invisible in an all-Owner fixture; the badge must read 4 while the table holds 7. **`MdsSwitch` is still I5's row** — the preferences card uses `MdsCheckbox`, and its two locked email controls are CHECKED + DISABLED + `aria-describedby` rather than switches wired to nothing (`honorsEmailPreference()` is false for `export_ready`/`webhook_failed`). **Three defects fixed that were not on the plan:** `GeneratePdfJob` records an `export_ready` row for EVERY terminal outcome, so a failed export said "Export ready" over a submission with no artifact (the email arm was always honest); the first spelling of that fix was `!== Ready`, which renders a MISSING outcome as a failure and contradicts its own sentence, caught by its own test; and `SubmissionInboxPresenter::pdfArtifact()`'s docblock had asserted "there is no notification bell, no polling" since before I3 merged.

**I3 (do not re-open):** **`review.requested` IS DELIBERATELY NOT A `DomainEventType` CASE, and adding one later would be a regression.** The I-map named it; there is no request-review verb in the product (`markUnderReview()` is a reviewer *claiming* a submission, the opposite), and that enum is ALSO the webhook + Slack **subscription** vocabulary — so a case with no emission site is a checkbox a tenant can tick that can never fire. `NotificationType::ReviewRequested` is a second fan-out of `submission.created` instead, targeted at reviewers, and `NotifyOnSubmissionCreated` SUBTRACTS the `submission_received` recipients from it so an Owner (who is in both role sets) gets ONE row per submission, not two. **The three cases I3 did add shipped with their six fan-out twins in the same commit** (`DispatchWebhooksFor*` + `DispatchConnectorsFor*`), for exactly the same reason — `WebhookFanOutTest` pins that a subscriber actually receives them. **Adding a `DomainEventType` case is a five-file act**, now written into the enum's own docblock: a migration recreating `webhook_deliveries_event_type_check` (else 23514 on the first delivery), a label — which since I3 lives ONCE on `DomainEventType::label()` rather than in the two presenters' byte-identical exhaustive `match`es — Slack copy + a deep link, a regenerated `openapi.json` (4 inline arrays; CI job 5 byte-diffs it), and the `eventTypes` count assertions, which are now `count(DomainEventType::cases())` rather than literals. **BOTH TABLES ARE `strict`, NOT `belongs_to_user`, AND THAT IS NOT STYLE** — `belongsToUserSql()` emits policies keyed only on `user_id` with NO tenant predicate, so a consultant in two tenants reads across both; `migration-lint` cannot catch it (it checks only that *some* `withTenantIsolation()` exists), so "mine" is the application predicate `scopeForUser()` and every read path must apply it. **`notifications` COLLIDES WITH LARAVEL'S OWN TABLE NAME** — `User` uses `Notifiable`, so `$user->unreadNotifications` now fails with `column notifications.notifiable_type does not exist`, which reads like a bug in this increment and is not; `'database'` is a forbidden channel repo-wide and `NotificationTableGuardTest` pins it. **`data` MAY NEVER HOLD A TOKEN OR A SIGNED URL** — under strict RLS every member of the tenant can read those rows and nothing redacts them (both tables are deliberately UNAUDITED, argued in audit-spec §1: a notification is a derivative of an already-audited act, and `read_at` churn would flood a ledger that is never pruned). **`MemberInvited` carries no accept URL and the invitation email did NOT move behind it** — the plaintext token exists only as a local in `invite()`, the URL must be built in-request under live tenant context, and the payload is webhook-visible, so an accept link there would be privilege escalation dressed as an integration; the event carries `{email, role, invited_by, expires_at}` and `email` is a **stated deviation** from webhook-design §3's default-exclude principle, recorded there rather than shipped silently. `returned_reason` is excluded from BOTH the webhook payload and the in-app row for the same principle. **`SubmissionReviewService::apply()` NO LONGER RETURNS `DB::transaction()` DIRECTLY** — an announcement inside the closure fires for a transition that then rolls back, and `DB::afterCommit` is verified never to fire under the suite's uncommitted outer transaction; `SubmissionReviewEventsTest` proves the ordering by reading `DB::transactionLevel()` from INSIDE the listener, which is the assertion `assertDispatched` cannot make. `markUnderReview`/`archive` announce NOTHING, deliberately, and the absence is asserted. **`ResourceGrantResolver` GAINED `primeFor()`, AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT AN OPTIMIZATION** — recipient resolution runs on a SYNCHRONOUS listener inside a public guest POST, so a per-candidate `holdsAny()` is one grant query per collaborator-scoped member in that request; the prime is one query and a bounded-query test (≤6 for 12 editors) is what stops a regression to per-user. It is a PRIME rather than a `granteesOfForm()` so the answer still comes from `decide()`, the one interpreter of `resource_grants`. Pass `holdsAny()` a **Form model, never an id** — the id branch re-anchors the form against the database on every call. **The recipient rule IS `SubmissionPolicy::view()`**, and `NotificationRecipientResolverTest` asserts agreement with `Gate::forUser()` itself rather than with a restated expectation: the bell must never announce a submission the inbox 403s on. Org-wide roles are DERIVED from `dashboard.org.view` in the permission catalog, never read off `RolePermissionSeeder::MATRIX` (app code reaching into a seeder for an authorization decision survives right up until someone edits the matrix). **`export_ready`/`webhook_failed` keep their purpose-built branded emails** and get an in-app row via `NotificationDispatcher::record()`, not `dispatch()` — `honorsEmailPreference()` is false for exactly those two, so their email is not preference-gated (both are operational and addressed to the one person who caused or owns the thing) and I4 must render those toggles as unavailable. **`EventNotification` is ONE class for the whole catalog** and needed BOTH registration sites (`QueuedMailContractTest`'s array AND `job-payload-lint`'s EXEMPT_JOBS) — two separate gates, and the linter test reads the script as a STRING, so the FQCN must match literally.

**I2 (do not re-open):** **`audits.auditable_id` IS KEYED ON THE OWNING TENANT OR USER WHENEVER THE TARGET'S PK IS NOT A UUID, AND THAT IS NOT DEFENSIVENESS.** `domains.id` is an `increments()` INTEGER (`create_domains_table.php:23`) while the column is `uuid NOT NULL` — a domain audit keyed on the domain row inserts `'3'` into a uuid column and **500s with `SQLSTATE 22P02` on the first live claim**, invisible to PHPStan, to the `audits_event_check` and to the migration linter. It is spec §1's role-grant device (`model_has_roles` has a composite PK and no surrogate id) applied a second time, it is written into §1 and data-dictionary §13 so nobody "fixes" it back, and **`AuditDomainSitesTest`'s first test is its only mechanical guard — mutation-verified**: reverting the key reddens with that exact error rather than a soft assertion. The hostname rides in `old_values`/`new_values.domain` because the key cannot carry it and `domains` rows are deletable, so a join would not always answer it either. **THE SECOND DOMAIN HAZARD IS THE ONE RLS CANNOT SEE.** `domains` is RLS-EXEMPT and `audits` is not, and the `audits` INSERT `WITH CHECK` compares `tenant_id` to the **AMBIENT GUC**, never to the row being described — so an operator path holding tenant B's domain under tenant A's context would **succeed** in filing B's event into A's ledger. `CustomDomainService::auditDomain()`'s `$tenantId !== $domain->tenant_id` check IS that isolation, exactly as the class docblock says of its `where('tenant_id', …)` filters. It also refuses when there is no context at all, and it **fails SILENT on purpose**: the sweep is a `$tries = 1` `MaintenanceJob`, so a throw there lands in `failed_jobs` on every tick forever — an audit gap is recoverable, a permanently-failing sweep is not. **`verify`/`activate`/`deactivate`/`sweep` ARE DELIBERATELY UNAUDITED and the tests pin the ABSENCE**, so a later reader cannot mistake a decision for an oversight: `verify()` is reached from HTTP *and* the console (a conditional audit would make the ledger depend on which caller won a race, and it records a check result the sweep rewrites every 15 minutes forever); `activate`/`deactivate` are artisan-only with **no actor**, so a row would carry `user_id = null` beside `is_system_action = false` — malformed by `AuditLogger`'s own contract. **`verification_token` STAYS OUT OF THE PAYLOAD AND OUT OF `AuditRedactor::SECRETS`** — the migration at `2026_08_04_000001…:49-51` already decided it is not a credential (it is published in public DNS at a name only the zone's controller can write); keeping a non-secret out of a payload is a different act from redacting it and must not drift into one. **`settings` ROWS ARE KEYED ON `tenants.id` PERMANENTLY, INCLUDING AFTER I5 CREATES THE `settings` TABLE** — pinned in spec §1 now precisely so I5 inherits the decision instead of making a silent one: settings are a per-tenant singleton, and keying new rows on a settings-row uuid would split one resource's history across two `auditable_id`s and make it unqueryable by `audits_tenant_auditable_idx`. Branding uses that same alias rather than a `branding` one (it renders inside `/settings` and gates on `tenant.settings.manage`; a second alias fragments one surface across two filter options), and **`brand_ramp` is never in a payload** — 2 KB of derived contrast tables that are a pure function of `primary_color`, in a table that is never pruned. **`submissions.remarks` IS REDACTED AND `returned_reason` IS NOT, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE DECISION:** remarks are internal free text with no audience discipline (a reviewer can paste respondent PII into them) and `audits` is never deleted, while the returned reason was authored FOR the respondent and already emailed to them — it is exactly the accountability the ledger exists to preserve. **THE §2.1 RENDERING RULE IS THE ONE MOST LIKELY TO BE "FIXED" INTO A DEFECT:** a redacted key already carries the placeholder on BOTH sides when it is written, so `AuditDiff` emits it verbatim on both and flags the key — it never reconstructs, nulls or hides it, and the UI shows a *Redacted* badge rather than printing `[REDACTED]` as if it were the value, above a notice that says the original was never recorded **including the "before" value**. **`is_system_action` IS DEAD DATA** — `AuditLogger` hard-codes it `false` and exposes no parameter, so it is false on 100% of rows; the page derives `is_system` from `user_id === null` instead, which is the condition that actually varies. The actor has **THREE** states, not two: `System` (null actor), a name, and **`Unknown user`** for a live non-null `user_id` whose row the join-shape `users` policy now hides because the membership was removed — rendering the last two identically would blame the platform for a person's act. **`event-variant.ts` IS A DELIBERATE DSR §3.8 DEVIATION**: it owns the badge COLOUR while `AuditEvent::label()` owns the LABEL (one string for the badge, the filter and the CSV). Do not fold it into `status-variant.ts` — `archived` is `warning` as a form STATE because an inert form needs attention, and an ordinary historical fact as an EVENT; amber on every such row spends the attention channel `exported` and `permission_changed` need, and `created`/`updated` in a map every screen reads would let a future status enum inherit an audit colour. **The export's self-referential row carries NO count** (the stream can be abandoned mid-body, so an intended count the client never received would be a false entry in the one table that exists not to contain those) and **the exported file contains its own export row** — accepted, pinned, and not to be tidied away by excluding `audit_log` rows, which would make the export lie about the ledger. **Two claims were WITHDRAWN after mutation testing refused to confirm them, and the withdrawals are in the code**: `applyLocal()`'s second argument does not keep the Actor column populated (the `users` policy resolves an active co-tenant through its membership branch with no user GUC), and `->with('user:id,name')` is an **N+1 guard no test enforces** because `data_get` lazy-loads. What IS verified: deleting `applyLocal()` empties the file at **HTTP 200**. **The URL is `/audit-log` and the page folder is `audit`** — the repo's first deliberate divergence (the URL matches the permission name and the PRD; the folder matches every sibling), so "fixing" either half breaks every `router.get` string on the page. **No column is `sortable`**: `MdsDataTable` sorts only the rows it was handed, so on a server-paginated ledger a sort header reorders 25 of 4,000 and lies (`webhooks/Show.vue` and `submissions/Inbox.vue` both carry that defect — noted, not copied, not fixed here). **The Filters `<h2>` is load-bearing**, not decoration: `PageHeader` renders the `<h1>` and `MdsEmptyState` an `<h3>`, so dropping it fails axe's heading-order **only in the empty state** — i.e. only on a database the seeder did not populate.

**I1 (do not re-open):** the QR is **server-rendered and that is not a preference** — `bacon/bacon-qr-code` v3.1.1 was ALREADY in `composer.lock` as a `laravel/fortify` dependency (Fortify server-renders the 2FA QR from it), and `XMLWriter` + `iconv` — its only requirements — were verified present in the container before the choice was made. Do not "modernize" it to a client-side npm library: that adds a production dependency under the `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` gate to redraw something that changes only when the author saves. It is a **direct `composer.json` require**, promoted with `composer update --lock` (a one-line content-hash diff, zero version movement) rather than `composer require` — leaving it transitive is how a `composer update` becomes an outage. It is delivered to an **`<img src>`, never `v-html`**: `TwoFactorSetup.vue:30` holds the only `v-html` in the entire `resources/` tree and is worth not making two of. **THE `frame-ancestors` ASYMMETRY IS THE DESIGN, AND "FIXING" IT BREAKS THE PRODUCT.** The public runtime states `frame-ancestors *` because PRD:187 *requires* third-party iframe embedding; the authenticated app states `frame-ancestors 'none'` via the new `AppSecurityHeaders`. **`AppSecurityHeaders` MUST NOT be mounted globally on `web`** — it is named on four groups explicitly (authenticated tenant app, invitations/branding, central platform pages, Fortify auth routes), because `web` also carries the guest runtime and a global mount would kill every embed in the product. **⚠️ THE DEFECT A TEST FOUND WAS MINE, AND IT WAS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ONE I PLANNED FOR.** `AppSecurityHeaders` **REPLACES** an inherited `frame-ancestors` rather than deferring to one.
